Prompt text (exact; client wrapper tags removed)
You are working on a new project called "AI QA Platform".

We are going to build this project from scratch based on the provided UI & API QA assignment.

IMPORTANT:
Do NOT implement any testing workflow yet.
Do NOT generate PRD, test cases, exploration, automation, or reports yet.

Your current task is ONLY to create the initial reusable platform architecture and project foundation.

==================================================
PROJECT GOAL
==================================================

Build a reusable AI-powered QA testing platform.

The platform must support:

1. Project/User Story/Task creation from a reusable template.
2. Requirement understanding and PRD generation.
3. UI and API exploration.
4. Test case design and test suite review.
5. Manual/AI test execution.
6. Playwright automation generation.
7. Automation execution.
8. Controlled self-healing.
9. Final reporting.
10. A complete AI QA Execution Journal that records every workflow step.

The solution must be reusable for future projects and tasks, not hardcoded only for OrangeHRM.

==================================================
CURRENT ASSIGNMENT
==================================================

The first project to validate the platform against is OrangeHRM.

UI:
https://opensource-demo.orangehrmlive.com/

API:
https://api.orangehrm.com/

The assignment requires these milestones:

Milestone 1:
Generate PRD using AI.
Input: Project URL
Output: PRD Markdown file.

Milestone 2:
Explore UI and API.
Input:
- Project URL
- PRD
- Selected coverage scope
Output:
- Exploration findings Markdown file.

Milestone 3:
Generate test cases CSV and review the correctness of the test suite.
The test design must contain:
- Valid in Scope
- Needs Automation

Milestone 4:
Execute ONLY test cases where:
Valid in Scope = TRUE

Generate an HTML execution report including bug reporting.

Milestone 5:
Generate Playwright automation ONLY for test cases where:
Needs Automation = TRUE

Run the automation using Claude and IntelliJ.

If automation requires healing/fixing:
- perform controlled healing
- document every healing attempt
- document the reason
- document the change
- document the result

Final deliverables:
- Excel milestone summary
- HTML workflow/sequence diagram
- PRD.md
- Exploration.md
- Test Design CSV
- Agent execution HTML report
- Playwright automation
- Automation execution HTML report
- Healing/fixing documentation

==================================================
REUSABLE TASK MODEL
==================================================

The platform must NOT create one fixed project structure for one task.

Instead:

Template:
platform/templates/task-template/

Task instances:
platform/tasks/<task-name>/

Example:

platform/tasks/login-invalid-credentials/
platform/tasks/add-employee/
platform/tasks/employee-search/

Every new task must be created by copying the reusable task template.

The template itself must never be modified during normal task execution.

==================================================
AI QA EXECUTION JOURNAL
==================================================

Every task must have an execution journal.

The journal must eventually capture:

- Step number
- Step name
- Timestamp
- Skill used
- Prompt used
- Prompt version
- Input
- Tools/MCP used
- Actions performed
- Output
- Files created/modified
- Validation result
- Errors
- Fixes
- Next step
- Evidence references

The purpose is educational and auditable.

A person opening the generated HTML journal should be able to understand:

"What prompt did the AI receive?"
"What did the AI do?"
"What tools did it use?"
"What did it produce?"
"Why did we move to the next step?"
"What happened when something failed?"
"How was it fixed?"

The journal should eventually generate an interactive HTML page.

==================================================
INITIAL DIRECTORY STRUCTURE
==================================================

Create the following structure:

AI-QA-Platform/
│
├── .claude/
│   ├── skills/
│   ├── prompts/

│

│   └── templates/
│
├── reports/
│
├── .mcp.json
├── CLAUDE.md

└── .gitignore

==================================================
TASK TEMPLATE
==================================================

Create:

platform/templates/task-template/

With this structure:

task-template/
│
├── context/
│   └── TaskContext.md
│
├── prd/

Do not generate actual task results yet.

WORKFLOW CONFIGURATION

- project-context

- manual-execution

- reporting

For now, mark them as planned/not implemented.

Do NOT create SKILL.md files yet.

We will implement them one by one later.

==================================================
CLAUDE.MD
==================================================

Create a root CLAUDE.md defining the platform principles.

Important principles:

1. Reusability
2. Task isolation
3. Evidence-based testing
4. No fabricated application behavior
5. UI/API exploration before test design
6. Test design must be reviewed
7. Valid in Scope controls execution
8. Needs Automation controls automation generation
9. Self-healing must never silently change expected behavior/assertions just to make tests pass
10. Every important AI action must eventually be journaled
11. Generated artifacts belong to the current task
12. Templates must remain immutable
13. Skills should be modular
14. Workflow stages should be independently replaceable

==================================================
README
==================================================

Create a README explaining:

- What the AI QA Platform is
- Why it exists
- The reusable Task Template concept
- The planned QA workflow
- The AI QA Execution Journal concept
- How future tasks will be created
- That OrangeHRM is the first validation project

Do not claim that any milestone has already been completed.

==================================================
IMPORTANT
==================================================

At the end:

1. Show the created directory tree.
2. Explain each top-level directory.
3. Show the workflow stages.
4. Show the skill registry.
5. Confirm that NO actual testing milestone has been executed yet.
6. Do not create fake PRD, exploration, test cases, execution results, bugs, or automation.

Prompt text (exact; client wrapper tags removed)
We are continuing the implementation of the AI QA Platform.

IMPORTANT:
I already have an existing Project Context skill specification that defines the intended behavior of this stage.

You MUST use that existing specification as the source of truth for the Project Context skill.

Do NOT replace its QA philosophy with a simpler implementation.
Do NOT redesign the stage from scratch.
Do NOT remove its scope decomposition, traceability, gaps, risks, assumptions, open questions, or approval gate.

The existing skill is based on:

PRD
  ↓
Project Context
  ↓
HARD STOP
  ↓
PROJECT_CONTEXT_APPROVED
  ↓
Next QA Stage

The existing specification is available in the provided SKILL.md reference and must be preserved conceptually.

==================================================
OBJECTIVE
==================================================

Create the production-ready reusable:

project-context

skill for the AI QA Platform.

The skill must be generic and reusable for different projects and different tasks.

It must NOT contain OrangeHRM-specific business logic.

OrangeHRM will only be used later as the first project that executes this workflow.

==================================================
EXISTING SKILL BEHAVIOR TO PRESERVE
==================================================

The Project Context stage must:

1. Read the current task's own PRD.
2. Read required shared knowledge-base material referenced by the PRD.
3. Read supporting material explicitly referenced by the PRD when required.
4. Read the PRD completely before proposing anything.
5. Perform Scope Decomposition.
6. Define task scope and boundaries.
7. Identify in-scope areas.
8. Identify explicitly out-of-scope areas.
9. Identify roles and permissions.
10. Extract business rules as independently verifiable statements.
11. Extract expected behaviors.
12. Identify dependencies.
13. Identify assumptions.
14. Identify risks.
15. Identify gaps and open questions.
16. Never invent requirements.
17. Never silently resolve unclear business rules.
18. Maintain traceability back to the PRD.
19. Write exactly one ProjectContext.md artifact for the current task.
20. Stop after producing the artifact.
21. Require the literal approval key:

PROJECT_CONTEXT_APPROVED

before the next stage is allowed to proceed.

==================================================
IMPORTANT SOURCE-OF-TRUTH RULE
==================================================

The Project Context skill must preserve this rule:

PRD is the source of documented task intent.

Observed application behavior belongs to the Exploration stage.

Therefore:

DO NOT use live UI/API exploration during Project Context.

DO NOT use Playwright MCP during this stage.

DO NOT infer undocumented behavior from the application.

DO NOT turn assumptions into requirements.

If something is missing or unclear in the PRD:

- record it as a GAP
- or record it as an OPEN QUESTION

Never guess.

==================================================
TASK STRUCTURE
==================================================

The current task instance is located under:

platform/tasks/<task-name>/

The task must already exist before this skill runs.

The Project Context output must be written to:

platform/tasks/<task-name>/context/ProjectContext.md

NEVER write task-specific content into:

platform/templates/task-template/

The template is immutable.

NEVER create a new task folder during this skill.

NEVER rename a task folder.

==================================================
EXPECTED PROJECT CONTEXT CONTENT
==================================================

The generated ProjectContext.md must contain:

# Project Context

## 1. Task Information

- Task ID
- Task Name
- Task Type
- Project Name
- Status

## 2. Scope Decomposition

For every identified functional area / flow:

- Name
- Description
- What it contains
- Why it is a distinct area/flow

## 3. Task Scope

### In Scope

Documented scope only.

### Explicitly Out of Scope

Only items explicitly excluded.

### Scope Boundaries

Explain important boundaries between this task and other functionality.

## 4. Roles and Permissions

For each role:

- Role
- Allowed actions
- Restricted actions

Only use information supported by the PRD.

## 5. Business Rules

Create numbered business rules.

Every rule must be independently verifiable.

Example:

BR-001
Description:
...

Source:
PRD section...

Do not invent rules.

## 6. Expected Behaviors

Document what the PRD explicitly says should happen.

Each behavior must be traceable to its PRD source.

## 7. Dependencies

Include:

- Other tasks
- Other modules
- External systems
- APIs
- Data dependencies
- Business dependencies

Only if supported by the available documentation.

## 8. Assumptions

Every assumption must be explicitly marked:

- Confirmed
- Unconfirmed

Never present an assumption as a fact.

## 9. Risks

Identify documented or logically derived QA risks.

For every risk:

- Risk ID
- Description
- Why it matters
- Related requirement/area
- Source

Clearly distinguish derived QA risk from documented requirement.

## 10. Gaps and Open Questions

Every gap/question must be specific.

Include:

- ID
- Question
- Related requirement/area
- Impact if known
- Owner only if explicitly provided

NEVER invent an owner.

## 11. Traceability

Map:

PRD Section
    ↓
Requirement
    ↓
Business Rule / Expected Behavior
    ↓
Scope Area

Maintain explicit references.

## 12. QA Scope Preparation

Prepare information for downstream stages:

### Areas requiring exploration

### UI areas requiring exploration

### API areas requiring exploration

### Business rules requiring validation

### Data requiring validation

IMPORTANT:

This section is only preparation.

Do NOT perform exploration.

==================================================
HARD STOP / APPROVAL GATE
==================================================

This is mandatory.

After ProjectContext.md is created:

STOP.

Do not:

- explore the application
- generate test cases
- execute tests
- generate automation
- prepare automation
- start another stage
- draft the next stage
- modify the PRD

The only valid progression key is:

PROJECT_CONTEXT_APPROVED

The following are NOT valid approval keys:

- looks good
- approved
- continue
- okay
- proceed
- LGTM

Only:

PROJECT_CONTEXT_APPROVED

unlocks the next stage.

If changes are requested:

1. Update the same ProjectContext.md file.
2. Do not create ProjectContext-v2.md.
3. Do not create ProjectContext-final.md.
4. Present the revised artifact.
5. STOP again.
6. Wait for PROJECT_CONTEXT_APPROVED.

==================================================
AI QA EXECUTION JOURNAL
==================================================

This is a CRITICAL requirement of the platform.

Every workflow stage must be observable and auditable.

The Project Context skill must therefore integrate with the AI QA Execution Journal.

The journal is intended to eventually generate an interactive HTML page that allows another person to understand:

- What step was executed?
- Why was the step executed?
- What prompt was used?
- What input was provided?
- What skill was used?
- What tools/MCP were used?
- What actions were performed?
- What output was produced?
- What files were created or modified?
- What assumptions were made?
- What gaps were found?
- What decisions were made?
- What validation happened?
- Why did the workflow stop?
- What approval was required?
- What happened in the next attempt if the user requested changes?

The journal is NOT a simple final report.

It is an execution history and learning artifact.

==================================================
JOURNAL EVENT
==================================================

Define a journal event for this skill.

The event must capture:

- event_id
- workflow_run_id
- task_id
- timestamp
- stage_id
- stage_name
- skill_name
- skill_version
- prompt_id
- prompt_version
- input_references
- source_references
- tools_used
- mcp_used
- actions
- output_artifacts
- assumptions
- gaps
- open_questions
- validation
- status
- approval_required
- approval_key
- next_stage
- errors
- notes

Do NOT fabricate runtime values.

Define the schema/template for the event.

==================================================

The platform must preserve the actual prompt used for this stage.

.claude/prompts/project-context.md

The prompt must have:

- Prompt ID

The execution journal must reference this prompt by:

prompt_id
prompt_version

However, the final HTML journal must be able to display the exact prompt version used for each execution.

==================================================
INPUT / OUTPUT EVIDENCE
==================================================

The journal must reference:

INPUT:

- Task PRD
- Relevant knowledge-base files
- Supporting referenced documents

OUTPUT:

- ProjectContext.md

If files are used, preserve their paths/references.

Do not fabricate evidence.

==================================================
MCP / TOOL LOGGING
==================================================

For Project Context:

Expected MCP usage:

None.

Playwright MCP must NOT be used.

The journal should explicitly record:

MCP:
None

This is important because the HTML journal must show not only what tools were used, but also when a tool was intentionally NOT used.

Prompt text (exact; client wrapper tags removed)
We are continuing the implementation of the AI QA Platform.

The Foundation and project-context skill have already been implemented.

We are now moving to the next workflow stage:

PRD GENERATION

Before implementing the PRD skill, apply the architecture decisions below.

==================================================
PART 1 — REQUIRED ARCHITECTURE FIXES
==================================================

Apply these fixes first.

--------------------------------------------------
FIX 1 — TaskContext.md vs ProjectContext.md
--------------------------------------------------

Keep these two artifacts separate.

TaskContext.md
=
Task Intake / Identity Metadata only.

It may contain:

- Task ID
- Task Name
- Task Type
- Project Name
- PRD path
- Task status
- Basic task metadata

It must NOT contain the detailed QA analysis.

ProjectContext.md
=
The output of the project-context skill.

It contains:

- Scope decomposition
- Roles and permissions
- Business rules
- Expected behaviors
- Dependencies
- Assumptions
- Risks
- Gaps
- Open questions
- Traceability
- QA scope preparation

Update the task template and any documentation/schema that currently conflicts with this separation.

Do not create duplicate responsibilities between the two files.

--------------------------------------------------
FIX 2 — Workflow Run ID ownership
--------------------------------------------------

The workflow/run orchestration layer owns:

workflow_run_id

Individual skills MUST NOT create the workflow_run_id.

A single workflow execution must have one workflow_run_id shared by all stages.

Example:

RUN-001
│
├── PRD Generation
├── Project Context
├── Exploration
├── Test Design
├── Test Review
├── Manual Execution
├── Automation Generation
├── Automation Execution
├── Self-Healing
└── Reporting

The Project Context skill and PRD skill must only READ the current workflow_run_id from the workflow context.

If no run ID exists, the skill must not invent one.

The workflow runner/orchestration layer is responsible for creating it.

Update schemas/documentation if necessary.

--------------------------------------------------
FIX 3 — PRD Approval Gate
--------------------------------------------------

Introduce an explicit PRD approval gate.

The workflow must become:

Task Creation
    ↓
PRD Generation
    ↓
HARD STOP
    ↓
PRD_APPROVED
    ↓
Project Context
    ↓
HARD STOP
    ↓
PROJECT_CONTEXT_APPROVED
    ↓
Exploration

Only the exact standalone key:

PRD_APPROVED

may unlock Project Context.

The following must NOT unlock the stage:

- approved
- looks good
- okay
- continue
- proceed
- LGTM
- any other text containing the key
- the key mixed with other text

The approval mechanism must follow the same strict behavior already used by Project Context.

If PRD changes are requested:

1. Update the same PRD.md.
2. Do not create PRD-v2.md.
3. Do not create PRD-final.md.
4. Record the revision in the journal.
5. Stop again.
6. Require PRD_APPROVED again.

==================================================
PART 2 — IMPLEMENT PRD GENERATION SKILL
==================================================

Create the reusable:

prd-generation

skill.

This skill must be generic and reusable across different:

- Web applications
- API projects
- Mobile applications
- UI + API projects
- Companies
- User stories
- Tasks

Do NOT hardcode OrangeHRM behavior.

OrangeHRM will be the first project used to validate this skill, but the skill itself must remain generic.

==================================================
OBJECTIVE
==================================================

The PRD Generation skill converts the initial task/project information into a structured Product Requirements Document that can be consumed by the Project Context stage.

Primary flow:

Task Input
    ↓
PRD Generation
    ↓
PRD.md
    ↓
HARD STOP
    ↓
PRD_APPROVED
    ↓
Project Context

The PRD is a requirements artifact.

It is NOT:

- an exploration report
- a test case document
- a bug report
- an automation plan
- an execution report

==================================================
INPUT CONTRACT
==================================================

The skill should consume the current task context.

Expected inputs:

1. Task ID
2. Task name
3. Task type
4. Project name
5. User Story / task description
6. Project UI URL if available
7. Project API URL if available
8. Acceptance criteria if provided
9. Scope if provided
10. Out-of-scope information if provided
11. Additional project information supplied by the user
12. workflow_run_id from the workflow orchestration layer

Task identity may be read from:

platform/tasks/<task-name>/context/TaskContext.md

The PRD skill must not create TaskContext.md.

==================================================
PROJECT URL HANDLING
==================================================

The assignment requires the project URL to be used as PRD input.

If a Project URL is provided:

- validate that the URL is present
- record the URL as a source
- use only information that can be reliably established from the available project/source information
- clearly distinguish source-derived facts from assumptions
- do not perform detailed UI exploration
- do not create UI exploration findings
- do not inspect the application deeply
- do not infer undocumented business behavior

The purpose of this stage is requirements generation, not application exploration.

Detailed UI/API exploration belongs to the Exploration stage.

If the URL cannot be accessed:

- do not fabricate information
- record the limitation
- continue using the supplied task/project information if sufficient
- identify the missing information as a gap/open question

==================================================
SOURCE-OF-TRUTH RULE
==================================================

Use this priority:

1. Explicit user/task requirements
2. Explicit acceptance criteria
3. Explicit project information
4. Verified source information from the provided project URL
5. Clearly labeled assumptions
6. Unknown / open questions

Never silently fill gaps.

Never invent:

- business rules
- roles
- permissions
- workflows
- API behavior
- validation rules
- database behavior
- UI behavior
- error messages
- expected results

If the information is not supported:

mark it as:

Unknown
or
Open Question

==================================================
PRD STRUCTURE
==================================================

Generate:

platform/tasks/<task-name>/prd/PRD.md

The PRD must contain the following sections.

# Product Requirements Document

## 1. Document Information

Include:

- PRD ID
- Task ID
- Task Name
- Project Name
- Task Type
- Version
- Status
- workflow_run_id
- Created timestamp
- Last updated timestamp

Do not fabricate timestamps or runtime identifiers.

Use actual values from the workflow/task context.

--------------------------------------------------

## 2. Source Information

Document:

- User/task requirement
- Project UI URL
- Project API URL if provided
- Supporting documents
- Other provided sources

Every important requirement must have a traceable source.

--------------------------------------------------

## 3. Problem Statement

Explain the problem described by the task.

Do not expand the problem beyond the provided information.

--------------------------------------------------

## 4. Goal

Describe what the task is intended to achieve.

Use the user's stated intent.

Do not invent business goals.

--------------------------------------------------

## 5. User Story

If provided:

As a...
I want...
So that...

If not provided:

Not provided.

--------------------------------------------------

## 6. Scope

### In Scope

Only documented scope.

### Out of Scope

Only explicitly documented exclusions.

### Scope Not Defined

Anything that cannot be determined from the available requirements.

--------------------------------------------------

## 7. Actors / Roles

Document roles explicitly mentioned by the requirements.

For each:

- Role
- Responsibility
- Relevant permissions if explicitly stated

Do not invent roles or permissions.

--------------------------------------------------

## 8. Functional Requirements

Create numbered requirements:

FR-001
FR-002
FR-003

Each requirement must be:

- clear
- independently understandable
- traceable
- testable where possible

Each requirement must include:

Requirement:
...

Source:
...

--------------------------------------------------

## 9. Business Rules

Extract business rules explicitly stated in the requirements.

Use IDs:

BR-001
BR-002
BR-003

Each rule must be independently verifiable.

Do not invent rules.

--------------------------------------------------

## 10. Acceptance Criteria

Preserve explicitly supplied acceptance criteria.

If acceptance criteria are not supplied:

state:

Acceptance criteria were not explicitly provided.

Do not invent acceptance criteria.

--------------------------------------------------

## 11. Data Requirements

Document only known data requirements.

Examples:

- Required input data
- Identifiers
- User data
- Business data
- Configuration data

Do not generate test data here.

--------------------------------------------------

## 12. UI Requirements

Document UI requirements only when explicitly supported by the source.

Do NOT perform detailed UI exploration.

Do NOT invent:

- selectors
- exact layouts
- field behavior
- validation messages
- UI components

--------------------------------------------------

## 13. API Requirements

Document API requirements only when explicitly supported.

Do NOT invent:

- endpoints
- request schemas
- response schemas
- authentication behavior
- status codes

If an API URL is provided but endpoint behavior is unknown:

record the API entry point and mark detailed API behavior as requiring exploration.

--------------------------------------------------

## 14. Non-Functional Requirements

Document explicitly provided:

- Performance requirements
- Security requirements
- Accessibility requirements
- Reliability requirements
- Compatibility requirements
- Availability requirements
- Other quality attributes

If none are provided:

state:

Not explicitly provided.

Do not invent values.

--------------------------------------------------

## 15. Dependencies

Document known dependencies.

Possible categories:

- Internal modules
- External systems
- APIs
- Authentication
- Data
- Configuration
- Other tasks

Only include supported dependencies.

--------------------------------------------------

## 16. Constraints

Document explicit constraints.

Examples:

- Technology constraints
- Business constraints
- Scope constraints
- Environment constraints
- Data constraints

Do not invent constraints.

--------------------------------------------------

## 17. Assumptions

Every assumption must be clearly labeled.

For each:

- ASSUMPTION ID
- Description
- Reason
- Source/context

Never present assumptions as requirements.

--------------------------------------------------

## 18. Gaps and Open Questions

For every unresolved point:

- GAP/QUESTION ID
- Description
- Related requirement
- Impact if known
- Owner only if explicitly provided

Never invent owners.

--------------------------------------------------

## 19. Traceability

Create traceability between:

Source
    ↓
Requirement
    ↓
Business Rule
    ↓
Acceptance Criterion

Where possible.

Every requirement should have a source.

--------------------------------------------------

## 20. QA Preparation Notes

This section is preparation for downstream QA stages.

Include:

- Areas that require exploration
- UI areas requiring validation
- API areas requiring validation
- Business rules requiring validation
- Data requiring validation
- Unknown behavior requiring investigation

IMPORTANT:

This is NOT exploration.

Do not claim that anything has been observed.

==================================================
PRD QUALITY RULES
==================================================

The PRD must be:

- structured
- traceable
- testable
- evidence-based
- reusable
- unambiguous where possible
- explicit about uncertainty

The PRD must never:

- invent requirements
- invent acceptance criteria
- invent application behavior
- invent API contracts
- invent business rules
- invent error messages
- silently resolve ambiguity

==================================================
NO EXPLORATION RULE
==================================================

This stage MUST NOT perform:

- browser exploration
- Playwright MCP exploration
- API endpoint discovery
- deep DOM inspection
- network analysis
- application workflow exploration

Those activities belong to:

exploration

The PRD stage may identify areas that need exploration.

It must not perform that exploration.

==================================================
APPROVAL GATE
==================================================

After PRD.md is created:

STOP.

Do not continue to Project Context automatically.

Do not:

- create ProjectContext.md
- explore the application
- generate test cases
- execute tests
- generate automation
- generate bugs
- generate reports

The only valid unlock key is:

PRD_APPROVED

The key must be provided as a standalone exact value.

Anything else must be rejected and journaled.

==================================================
REVISION HANDLING
==================================================

If the reviewer requests changes:

1. Keep the same PRD.md.
2. Update the existing document.
3. Increment the PRD version.
4. Record the revision reason.
5. Record the changed sections.
6. Record the new prompt version if the prompt itself changed.
7. Create a journal revision event.
8. Stop again.
9. Require PRD_APPROVED again.

Example:

PRD v1.0.0
    ↓
Review
    ↓
Change requested
    ↓
PRD v1.1.0
    ↓
HARD STOP
    ↓
PRD_APPROVED

Do not create multiple PRD files for revisions.

==================================================
PROMPT
==================================================

Create:

.claude/prompts/prd-generation.md

The prompt must be versioned.

Initial version:

prd-generation
v1.0.0

The prompt file must contain:

- prompt_id
- prompt_version
- purpose
- expected inputs
- source-of-truth rules
- generation instructions
- PRD structure
- quality rules
- stop conditions
- approval requirements

The prompt must be generic.

Do not include OrangeHRM-specific instructions.

==================================================
SKILL
==================================================

Create:

.claude/skills/prd-generation/SKILL.md

The skill must define:

- Name
- Purpose
- Trigger conditions
- Inputs
- Outputs

- PRD structure

- Hard-stop behavior
- Approval gate
- Journal integration
- Traceability

The skill must be detailed enough for another AI agent to execute without undocumented instructions.

Do not create a second journal architecture.

Use the existing:

platform/schemas/journal-event.schema.json

and:

platform/schemas/prompt-record.schema.json

Use the existing task journal:

and:

platform/tasks/<task-name>/journal/prompts.json

==================================================
PROMPT RECORDING
==================================================

The exact prompt used must be recoverable later.

When a prompt version is first used for a task:

- store the prompt record
- store the exact prompt text
- store its SHA-256 hash
- store prompt_id
- store prompt_version

Later journal events should reference the prompt by ID/version instead of duplicating the entire prompt.

The final HTML journal must be able to display the exact prompt used.

For PRD Generation:

If the PRD stage accesses the provided project URL through an available source/tool, record exactly what was used.

If no external tool is used:

record:

None

Prompt text (exact; client wrapper tags removed)
We are continuing the implementation of the AI QA Platform.

IMPORTANT:
We are now implementing the EXPLORATION stage.

I have an existing Explorer SKILL.md specification that defines the intended QA behavior for this stage.

The existing Explorer specification is the source of truth for the core exploration behavior.

You MUST recreate that Explorer skill from scratch inside the NEW AI QA Platform architecture while preserving its original QA philosophy, responsibilities, classifications, evidence requirements, and approval behavior.

Do NOT simplify the existing Explorer logic.

Do NOT replace it with a generic browser-testing skill.

Do NOT remove any of its existing exploration responsibilities.

At the same time, upgrade the skill so it fully integrates with the architecture we have already established for:

- Project Context
- PRD Generation
- Workflow orchestration
- Workflow Run ID
- Prompt versioning
- Journal events
- Evidence
- Approval gates
- Future interactive HTML QA Journal

The final result must be a production-ready reusable Exploration Skill.

==================================================
EXISTING EXPLORER BEHAVIOR — PRESERVE THIS
==================================================

The Explorer stage is responsible for learning how the application actually behaves before test cases are written.

Core flow:

APPROVED PROJECT CONTEXT
        +
LIVE APPLICATION
        ↓
EXPLORATION
        ↓
EXPECTED VS ACTUAL
        ↓
EXPLORATION REPORT
        ↓
HUMAN REVIEW
        ↓
EXPLORATION_APPROVED

The existing Explorer skill must preserve these principles:

1. Exploration happens only after Project Context approval.

2. The Explorer compares documented expected behavior against actual application behavior.

3. The Explorer records what was actually observed.

4. The Explorer must not invent requirements.

5. Undocumented behavior must not automatically become a requirement.

6. The Explorer must classify observations.

7. Evidence must be captured for non-MATCH observations.

8. Potential bugs must not automatically become confirmed bugs.

9. Test cases must NOT be created during exploration.

10. The stage must HARD STOP after the Exploration Report.

11. The next stage is unlocked only by:

EXPLORATION_APPROVED

==================================================
NEW PLATFORM ARCHITECTURE
==================================================

The platform now uses:

.claude/
    skills/
    prompts/

platform/
    knowledge-base/
    registry/
    schemas/
    tasks/
    templates/
    workflows/

reports/

Do NOT use the old platform_core_data/modules structure.

The new task structure is:

platform/tasks/<task-name>/

The Exploration output must be:

platform/tasks/<task-name>/exploration/ExplorationReport.md

The approved Project Context input must be:

platform/tasks/<task-name>/context/ProjectContext.md

The task template remains:

platform/templates/task-template/

Never write runtime task data into the template.

==================================================
WORKFLOW POSITION
==================================================

The current workflow is:

TASK CREATION
      ↓
PRD GENERATION
      ↓
PRD_APPROVED
      ↓
PROJECT CONTEXT
      ↓
PROJECT_CONTEXT_APPROVED
      ↓
EXPLORATION
      ↓
EXPLORATION_APPROVED
      ↓
TEST DESIGN

Exploration is therefore downstream of Project Context and upstream of Test Design.

The workflow orchestration layer owns workflow_run_id.

The Exploration skill must NOT create a workflow_run_id.

It must read the active workflow_run_id from the orchestration context.

All stages belonging to one workflow execution must share the same workflow_run_id.

Example:

RUN-001
│
├── PRD Generation
├── Project Context
├── Exploration
├── Test Design
├── Test Review
├── Manual Execution
├── Automation Generation
├── Automation Execution
├── Self-Healing
└── Reporting

==================================================
CREATE THE SKILL
==================================================

Create:

.claude/skills/exploration/SKILL.md

The skill must contain:

- name
- description
- purpose
- stage identity
- trigger conditions
- prerequisites
- inputs
- outputs
- tooling
- responsibilities
- non-responsibilities
- exploration strategy
- scope control
- expected vs actual rules
- observation classification
- evidence rules
- candidate locator rules
- API exploration rules if applicable
- UI exploration rules
- workflow exploration rules
- validation rules
- failure conditions
- revision behavior
- hard-stop behavior
- approval gate
- journal integration
- traceability
- HTML journal observability requirements

==================================================
SKILL IDENTITY
==================================================

Use:

name: exploration

The description should communicate that this skill:

- explores the live application
- uses Playwright MCP for browser access
- works against an approved ProjectContext
- compares documented expected behavior with actual behavior
- classifies observations
- records evidence
- produces ExplorationReport.md
- stops for human review

Do not use stage numbers such as:

Stage 1
Stage 2
Stage 3

inside the core skill contract.

Use:

stage_id: exploration

The workflow controls ordering.

==================================================
PREREQUISITES
==================================================

The skill MUST NOT start unless:

1. A task exists.
2. The task has a valid TaskContext.md.
3. The task has a PRD.md.
4. ProjectContext.md exists.
5. Project Context has been explicitly approved using:

PROJECT_CONTEXT_APPROVED

6. The approval is current and valid for the current ProjectContext version/hash.
7. An active workflow_run_id exists.

If any prerequisite is missing:

- do not explore
- do not use Playwright MCP
- do not create an ExplorationReport
- create an appropriate journal failure/blocked event
- stop

==================================================
APPROVED PROJECT CONTEXT
==================================================

The primary exploration input is:

platform/tasks/<task-name>/context/ProjectContext.md

The Explorer must use the APPROVED ProjectContext as the documented expectation baseline.

The Explorer must understand:

- in-scope areas
- out-of-scope areas
- roles
- permissions
- business rules
- expected behaviors
- dependencies
- risks
- gaps
- open questions
- QA exploration preparation notes

Do not explore unrelated areas unless the exploration itself reveals a dependency that must be followed to understand an in-scope flow.

If additional exploration becomes necessary:

record why it was necessary.

==================================================
SOURCE-OF-TRUTH RULE
==================================================

The Explorer must distinguish between:

DOCUMENTED EXPECTATION
and
OBSERVED ACTUAL BEHAVIOR

Use:

ProjectContext
    =
Expected / documented behavior

Live application
    =
Actual / observed behavior

Do not rewrite the ProjectContext to match what the application does.

Do not assume the application is correct.

Do not assume the documentation is correct.

Record the difference.

Example:

Expected:
User must provide a valid email address.

Actual:
Application accepts an invalid email format.

Classification:
FINDING

Do NOT automatically call it a bug.

==================================================
NO INVENTED REQUIREMENTS
==================================================

Never invent requirements to explain observed behavior.

If the application contains behavior that is not documented:

classify it as:

GAP

or:

QUESTION

depending on whether the missing requirement is simply undocumented or requires human/business clarification.

Do not convert observations into business rules.

==================================================
TOOLING
==================================================

Use the Playwright MCP server for browser access during this stage.

Playwright MCP is a:

BROWSER ACCESS AND EXPLORATION TOOL.

It is NOT the automation framework.

Do not generate automation code.

Do not create Selenium code.

Do not create SHAFT code.

Do not create TestNG code.

Do not create Playwright automation test code.

The Playwright MCP interaction is exploration only.

==================================================
PLAYWRIGHT EXPLORATION RULES
==================================================

Use Playwright MCP to:

- open the application
- navigate relevant pages
- interact with relevant UI
- inspect visible fields
- inspect dropdowns
- inspect validation behavior
- inspect dependencies
- follow relevant workflows
- observe navigation
- inspect relevant element attributes
- capture screenshots/evidence where supported
- capture accessibility snapshots where supported
- collect candidate locator information

Do NOT use Playwright to create automation scripts.

Do NOT use exploration as an excuse to explore the entire application without scope.

==================================================
EXPLORATION SCOPE
==================================================

Exploration must be scope-driven.

Start from:

ProjectContext
    ↓
Areas requiring exploration
    ↓
Exploration plan
    ↓
Actual exploration

For each area:

1. Identify the expected behavior.
2. Identify the relevant UI/API flow.
3. Explore the flow.
4. Record actual behavior.
5. Compare expected vs actual.
6. Classify the observation.
7. Capture evidence when required.

Do not randomly click through the application.

==================================================
RESPONSIBILITIES
==================================================

The Explorer must inspect, where applicable:

### Fields

- field type
- required/optional state
- format
- limits
- defaults
- visible behavior
- validation behavior

### Dropdowns

- actual options
- ordering
- default state
- empty state
- dynamically loaded options where observable

### Validations

- when validation fires
- exact message text
- client-side/server-side behavior when observable
- behavior after invalid input
- behavior after correction

### Dependencies

- fields that change based on other fields
- sections that appear/disappear
- values that enable/disable other controls
- dependencies between screens

### Workflows

- actual end-to-end path
- states
- transitions
- navigation
- success behavior
- failure behavior

### Relevant APIs

Only when API behavior is explicitly in scope or needed to understand the UI flow.

Record observed API behavior as observation/evidence.

Do not invent undocumented API contracts.

==================================================
OBSERVATION MODEL
==================================================

Every meaningful observation must have:

- Observation ID
- Area
- Expected behavior
- Actual behavior
- Classification
- Evidence reference
- Source reference where applicable
- Notes

Every observation must be traceable.

==================================================
OBSERVATION CLASSIFICATION
==================================================

Every observation gets EXACTLY ONE classification.

Use these classifications:

MATCH
FINDING
GAP
QUESTION
POTENTIAL BUG

Definitions:

MATCH
=
Actual behavior matches the documented expected behavior.

FINDING
=
Actual behavior differs from the documented expected behavior.

GAP
=
The expected behavior is missing or undocumented.

QUESTION
=
Human/business clarification is required before the behavior can be judged.

POTENTIAL BUG
=
The behavior appears incorrect, but confirmation is required before calling it a defect.

==================================================
CLASSIFICATION RULES
==================================================

A difference from documented behavior is:

FINDING

not automatically:

BUG

The Explorer must NOT upgrade:

POTENTIAL BUG

into:

CONFIRMED BUG

Formal bug reporting belongs to a later QA stage.

If the ProjectContext is silent about a behavior:

GAP

Do not decide what the requirement should be.

If business clarification is required:

QUESTION

==================================================
EVIDENCE
==================================================

Capture evidence for anything that is not a simple MATCH.

Possible evidence:

- screenshot
- accessibility snapshot
- relevant element attributes
- URL
- page title
- relevant API request/response evidence if available
- browser state
- other supported evidence

Every evidence item must have:

- evidence ID
- type
- source/action
- related observation
- file/reference if available
- timestamp if available from the real system

Never fabricate evidence.

If evidence cannot be captured:

record why.

==================================================
CANDIDATE LOCATORS
==================================================

The Explorer may record candidate locators for future automation.

For each relevant element record:

- element description
- stable ID if available
- meaningful attribute usable in XPath
- locator notes
- stability observations

IMPORTANT:

Candidate locators are NOT automation code.

Do not write:

- Selenium code
- SHAFT code
- TestNG code
- Playwright test code

The downstream automation stage will decide how to implement the locator.

For this platform's automation strategy, future automation will use:

Selenium + SHAFT + Java + TestNG

with ID/XPath locator preference.

Do not implement that automation here.

==================================================
EXPECTED VS ACTUAL
==================================================

Every comparison must maintain:

Expected:
[Documented expectation]

Actual:
[Observed application behavior]

Classification:
[MATCH/FINDING/GAP/QUESTION/POTENTIAL BUG]

Evidence:
[Evidence reference]

Source:
[ProjectContext requirement/business rule/expected behavior reference]

Never write an actual behavior into the Expected field.

Never write an expectation into the Actual field.

==================================================
EXPLORATION REPORT
==================================================

Create:

platform/tasks/<task-name>/exploration/ExplorationReport.md

The report must contain:

# Exploration Report

## 1. Exploration Information

- Task ID
- Task Name
- Project Name
- Environment
- Application URL
- Workflow Run ID
- Exploration start/end information if available
- Skill version
- Prompt version

## 2. Exploration Scope

Document:

- areas explored
- flows explored
- areas intentionally not explored
- reasons for exclusions

## 3. Environment

Document:

- application URL
- environment
- relevant configuration if known
- exploration date/time from the real system

Do not fabricate environment information.

## 4. Exploration Method

- expected vs actual comparison was performed
- evidence was captured

|----|------|----------|--------|----------------|----------|--------|

- evidence

- behavior

- evidence

Summarize:

- matches

- coverage limitations

Do not provide an overall quality verdict.

The only valid progression key is:

- exact
- case-sensitive
- standalone

These are NOT valid:

- any message containing the key with additional text

8. Update the report version.

10. Require EXPLORATION_APPROVED again.

If the approved Exploration Report is changed:

the previous approval becomes invalid.

Prompt text (exact; client wrapper tags removed)
We are continuing the implementation of the AI QA Platform.

IMPORTANT:

We are now implementing the TEST DESIGN stage.

I have an existing Test Design SKILL.md specification.

The existing Test Design specification is the SOURCE OF TRUTH for the core test-design behavior.

You MUST recreate the Test Design skill from scratch inside the NEW AI QA Platform architecture while preserving its original:

- purpose
- responsibilities
- test design techniques
- coverage model
- test case structure
- traceability rules
- exploratory case rules
- open question rules
- coverage gap rules
- test data requirements
- review gate
- approval behavior

Do NOT replace it with a generic test-case generator.

Do NOT simplify the existing Test Design logic.

Do NOT remove any of its systematic test-design techniques.

At the same time, upgrade it so it fully integrates with the architecture already established for:

- PRD Generation
- Project Context
- Exploration
- Workflow orchestration
- Workflow Run ID
- Prompt versioning
- Journal events
- Evidence
- Traceability
- Future interactive HTML QA Journal

==================================================
ORIGINAL TEST DESIGN BEHAVIOR — PRESERVE
==================================================

The original Test Design flow is:

APPROVED PROJECT CONTEXT
        +
APPROVED EXPLORATION REPORT
        ↓
TEST DESIGN
        ↓
TEST CASES
        +
TRACEABILITY
        +
TECHNIQUE RATIONALE
        +
EXPLORATORY CASES
        +
OPEN QUESTIONS
        +
COVERAGE GAPS
        +
TEST DATA REQUIREMENTS
        ↓
HUMAN REVIEW
        ↓
TEST_DESIGN_APPROVED

The Test Design stage must remain a DESIGN stage.

It does NOT execute tests.

It does NOT open the browser.

It does NOT use Playwright MCP for live exploration.

It does NOT generate automation code.

It does NOT create confirmed bug reports.

==================================================
NEW PLATFORM ARCHITECTURE
==================================================

The platform uses:

.claude/
    skills/
    prompts/

platform/
    knowledge-base/
    registry/
    schemas/
    tasks/
    templates/
    workflows/

reports/

The task structure is:

platform/tasks/<task-name>/

The Test Design output must be:

platform/tasks/<task-name>/test-design/TestCases.md

The task template is:

platform/templates/task-template/

Never write runtime task data into the template.

==================================================
WORKFLOW POSITION
==================================================

The workflow is:

TASK CREATION
      ↓
PRD GENERATION
      ↓
PRD_APPROVED
      ↓
PROJECT CONTEXT
      ↓
PROJECT_CONTEXT_APPROVED
      ↓
EXPLORATION
      ↓
EXPLORATION_APPROVED
      ↓
TEST DESIGN
      ↓
TEST_DESIGN_APPROVED
      ↓
MANUAL EXECUTION
      ↓
AUTOMATION GENERATION
      ↓
AUTOMATION EXECUTION
      ↓
SELF HEALING
      ↓
REPORTING

The workflow orchestration layer owns workflow_run_id.

The Test Design skill MUST NOT create workflow_run_id.

It must read the active workflow_run_id from:

platform/tasks/<task-name>/journal/workflow-run.json

If workflow_run_id is missing:

- do not generate test cases
- record a blocked journal event
- stop

==================================================
CREATE THE SKILL
==================================================

Create:

.claude/skills/test-design/SKILL.md

The skill must contain:

- name
- description
- stage identity
- purpose
- trigger conditions
- prerequisites
- inputs
- outputs
- responsibilities
- non-responsibilities
- test design strategy
- technique selection rules
- coverage rules
- test case generation rules
- traceability rules
- exploratory case rules
- open question rules
- coverage gap rules
- test data rules
- validation rules
- revision behavior
- hard-stop behavior
- approval gate
- journal integration
- HTML observability requirements

Use:

stage_id: test-design

Do NOT use stage numbers as the identity.

The workflow controls ordering.

==================================================
PREREQUISITES
==================================================

The Test Design stage MUST NOT start unless:

1. A task exists.
2. TaskContext.md exists.
3. PRD.md exists.
4. ProjectContext.md exists.
5. ProjectContext has valid:

PROJECT_CONTEXT_APPROVED

6. ProjectContext approval is current.
7. ExplorationReport.md exists.
8. ExplorationReport has valid:

EXPLORATION_APPROVED

9. Exploration approval is current.
10. The approved Exploration Report corresponds to the current approved ProjectContext.
11. An active workflow_run_id exists.

If any prerequisite fails:

- do not generate TestCases.md
- do not generate test cases
- do not use Playwright MCP
- record a blocked journal event
- stop

==================================================
INPUTS
==================================================

Primary inputs:

1. Approved:

platform/tasks/<task-name>/context/ProjectContext.md

2. Approved:

platform/tasks/<task-name>/exploration/ExplorationReport.md

3. TaskContext.md for task identity only.

4. Relevant PRD references where traceability requires them.

5. Test-data constraints and environment constraints if provided.

==================================================
SOURCE OF TRUTH
==================================================

Use:

ProjectContext
    =
Documented scope and business rules

ExplorationReport
    =
Observed application behavior

Test Design
    =
Test coverage derived from both approved sources

Do NOT treat ExplorationReport as a replacement for ProjectContext.

Do NOT treat undocumented behavior as a confirmed requirement.

Do NOT invent expected results.

==================================================
CRITICAL RULE — NO INVENTED EXPECTED RESULTS
==================================================

Never invent an expected result for undocumented behavior.

If a behavior has no established expected result:

DO NOT guess.

Instead classify the resulting test case as:

Exploratory

or create:

Open Question

The test case must explicitly state what needs to be clarified.

Example:

Observed:
Application accepts an undocumented value.

Incorrect:

Expected:
The application should reject it.

Correct:

Expected:
Not established by approved requirements.

Classification:
Exploratory

Open Question:
Should this value be accepted or rejected?

==================================================
TEST DESIGN TECHNIQUES
==================================================

The skill must systematically select and apply appropriate techniques.

Supported techniques:

1. Equivalence Partitioning
2. Boundary Value Analysis
3. Decision Tables
4. State Transition Testing
5. Pairwise / Combinatorial Testing
6. Error Guessing

The skill must NOT apply every technique blindly.

For each behavior:

1. Identify the behavior/rule.
2. Determine which technique is appropriate.
3. Apply the technique.
4. Explain why it was selected.
5. Generate the minimum defensible set of cases.
6. Verify coverage.

==================================================
EQUIVALENCE PARTITIONING
==================================================

Use equivalence partitioning where inputs can be divided into logically equivalent classes.

Examples:

- valid
- invalid
- missing
- malformed

Record:

- partition
- representative value
- expected behavior
- source

Do not generate redundant cases without justification.

==================================================
BOUNDARY VALUE ANALYSIS
==================================================

For every documented numeric, length, date, quantity, or similar limit:

consider:

- just below boundary
- boundary
- just above boundary

Where applicable also consider:

- minimum
- minimum + 1
- maximum - 1
- maximum
- maximum + 1

Only generate boundaries supported by approved requirements or approved observed behavior.

==================================================
DECISION TABLES
==================================================

Use decision tables where behavior depends on multiple conditions.

Document:

- conditions
- condition values
- resulting actions
- covered combinations
- test case IDs

Avoid unnecessary combinations where equivalent outcomes can be justified.

==================================================
STATE TRANSITION TESTING
==================================================

Use state transition testing where the application contains lifecycle/state behavior.

Examples:

- Draft → Submitted
- Pending → Approved
- Pending → Rejected
- Active → Suspended
- Booked → Cancelled

Cover valid transitions and invalid transitions where requirements or observed behavior establish them.

Do not invent state transitions.

==================================================
PAIRWISE / COMBINATORIAL TESTING
==================================================

Use pairwise testing when multiple independent parameters create a large combination space.

Document:

- dimensions
- values
- pairwise strategy
- selected combinations
- coverage achieved

Do not claim exhaustive coverage when only pairwise coverage was applied.

==================================================
ERROR GUESSING
==================================================

Use error guessing only as a supplementary technique.

It must never replace systematic techniques.

Possible areas:

- missing required data
- malformed input
- repeated submission
- unexpected sequence
- timeout
- duplicate action
- invalid state
- stale data

Document the rationale.

==================================================
COVERAGE MODEL
==================================================

Where applicable, cover:

1. Positive
2. Negative
3. Boundary
4. Validation
5. Business Rules
6. Dependencies
7. Error Handling
8. Exploratory
9. End-to-End

Every applicable business rule must have at least one covering test case.

Every relevant Exploration observation must be considered.

Not every observation necessarily requires a test case.

If an observation does not require testing:

record why.

==================================================
TEST CASE STRUCTURE
==================================================

Every test case must contain:

- Test Case ID
- Title
- Type
- Priority
- Preconditions
- Test Data
- Steps
- Expected Result
- Source / Traceability

Each test case must have:

ONE single unambiguous expected result.

Do not combine multiple independent expectations into one expected-result field.

Test cases must be:

- independent
- repeatable
- executable
- deterministic where possible

Do not rely on test execution order.

==================================================
TEST CASE TYPES
==================================================

Use clear types such as:

- Positive
- Negative
- Boundary
- Validation
- Business Rule
- Dependency
- Error Handling
- Exploratory
- End-to-End

Do not invent unnecessary categories.

==================================================
TEST CASE IDs
==================================================

Use a stable test case ID format.

Example:

TC-001
TC-002
TC-003

IDs must remain stable during revisions.

If a test case is edited, preserve its ID.

If a test case is removed:

record that it was withdrawn and why.

Do not renumber existing cases unnecessarily.

==================================================
PRIORITY
==================================================

Each test case must have a priority.

Use:

High
Medium
Low

Priority must be based on documented business importance, risk, critical workflow impact, or explicit project context.

Do NOT invent business priority when no evidence exists.

If priority cannot be established:

record:

Not established

or use the project's defined default only if one exists.

==================================================
TRACEABILITY
==================================================

Every test case must trace back to at least one approved source.

Possible source types:

Project Context rule
Exploration observation
Business rule
Documented requirement

Use stable source IDs.

Example:

PC-BR-001
OBS-014

Do not invent source IDs.

If the upstream document already has an ID, reuse it.

Maintain:

Source
    ↓
Test Case

And also:

Test Case
    ↓
Source

==================================================
TRACEABILITY MATRIX
==================================================

The TestCases.md must include:

| Source ID | Source Type | Description | Test Cases |
|-----------|-------------|-------------|------------|

Every approved business rule must appear in the matrix.

Every relevant Exploration observation must be considered.

Uncovered sources must be explicitly identified.

==================================================
EXPLORATORY CASES
==================================================

Exploratory cases are allowed only where:

- behavior is undocumented
- expected result is not established
- the Exploration Report identifies a GAP
- further investigation is required

Every exploratory case must include:

- test case ID
- reason it is exploratory
- observed behavior
- what is unknown
- question that must be answered
- evidence/source reference

Do NOT convert a GAP into a guessed expected result.

==================================================
OPEN QUESTIONS
==================================================

Maintain an Open Questions section.

For every question:

- Question ID
- related source
- related test cases
- exact ambiguity
- required clarification

Questions must remain unresolved until the appropriate human/business decision is provided.

==================================================
COVERAGE GAPS
==================================================

Explicitly document:

- uncovered business rules
- uncovered observations
- inaccessible functionality
- missing test data
- unavailable roles
- environment limitations
- intentionally excluded areas

For every gap:

- reason
- impact if known
- dependency/blocker
- whether follow-up is required

Do not hide missing coverage.

==================================================
TEST DATA REQUIREMENTS
==================================================

Document data required for execution.

Examples:

- valid users
- invalid users
- roles
- dates
- numeric ranges
- account states
- booking data
- dependent records

Do not fabricate real credentials.

Do not include secrets.

If credentials are required:

reference the configured test-data source.

==================================================
EXPECTED RESULT RULE
==================================================

Expected results must be supported by:

1. ProjectContext
2. Approved Exploration
3. Explicit documented business rule

If none supports the expected result:

DO NOT invent it.

Mark:

Exploratory

or:

Open Question

==================================================
NO EXECUTION
==================================================

The Test Design skill must NEVER:

- open a browser
- execute test cases
- use Playwright MCP for live exploration
- generate Selenium code
- generate SHAFT code
- generate TestNG code
- generate automation scripts
- report actual pass/fail execution results
- create confirmed bugs

Those belong to later stages.

We are continuing the implementation of the AI QA Platform.

IMPORTANT:

We are now implementing the MANUAL EXECUTION stage.

I have an existing Manual Execution SKILL.md specification.

The existing Manual Execution specification is the SOURCE OF TRUTH for the core execution behavior.

You MUST recreate the Manual Execution skill from scratch inside the NEW AI QA Platform architecture while preserving its original:

- purpose
- execution behavior
- approved test case requirement
- exact-step execution rule
- expected vs actual model
- PASS / FAIL / BLOCKED statuses
- evidence requirements
- failure analysis
- defect/root-cause analysis
- distinct-defect bug grouping
- severity and priority separation
- execution reporting
- bug reporting
- hard-stop behavior
- MANUAL_EXECUTION_APPROVED gate
- in-place revision behavior

Do NOT replace it with a generic test runner.

Do NOT simplify the original execution logic.

Do NOT automatically fix failures.

Do NOT reinterpret test cases during execution.

Do NOT generate automation code.

At the same time, upgrade the skill so it fully integrates with the architecture already established for:

- PRD Generation
- Project Context
- Exploration
- Test Design
- Workflow orchestration
- Workflow Run ID
- Prompt versioning
- Journal events
- Evidence
- Traceability
- Future interactive HTML QA Journal

==================================================
ORIGINAL MANUAL EXECUTION BEHAVIOR — PRESERVE
==================================================

The original execution flow is:

APPROVED TEST CASES
        ↓
LIVE APPLICATION
        ↓
EXECUTE EXACT STEPS
        ↓
EXPECTED VS ACTUAL
        ↓
PASS / FAIL / BLOCKED
        ↓
FAILURE ANALYSIS
        ↓
BUG REPORTS FOR DISTINCT DEFECTS
        ↓
EXECUTION REPORT
        ↓
HUMAN REVIEW
        ↓
MANUAL_EXECUTION_APPROVED

The Manual Execution stage must execute ONLY approved test cases.

It must never invent a test case during execution.

If a required test case does not exist:

STOP and record the issue.

Do not create a new test case during execution.

==================================================
NEW PLATFORM ARCHITECTURE
==================================================

The platform uses:

.claude/
    skills/
    prompts/

    knowledge-base/
    registry/
    schemas/
    tasks/
    templates/
    workflows/

reports/

platform/tasks/<task-name>/

Do NOT use the old:

platform_core_data/modules/

paths.

Runtime artifacts must live under:

platform/tasks/<task-name>/

==================================================

==================================================

The workflow is:

      ↓

      ↓

      ↓

      ↓

      ↓

      ↓

The workflow orchestration layer owns workflow_run_id.

The Manual Execution skill MUST NOT create workflow_run_id.

platform/tasks/<task-name>/journal/workflow-run.json

If workflow_run_id is missing:

- do not execute tests
- record a blocked journal event
- do not use Playwright MCP
- stop

==================================================

==================================================

Create:

.claude/skills/manual-execution/SKILL.md

- severity rules

- execution report rules
- coverage rules
- revision behavior
- hard-stop behavior
- approval gate
- journal integration
- HTML observability requirements

Use:

stage_id: manual-execution

Do NOT use stage numbers as the identity.

The workflow controls ordering.

==================================================
PREREQUISITES
==================================================

The Manual Execution stage MUST NOT start unless:

1. A task exists.
2. TaskContext.md exists.
3. PRD.md exists.
4. ProjectContext.md exists.
5. ProjectContext approval is valid.
6. ExplorationReport.md exists.
7. Exploration approval is valid.
8. TestCases.md exists.
9. Test Design approval is valid:

TEST_DESIGN_APPROVED

10. The approved TestCases.md corresponds to the current approved upstream artifacts.
11. An active workflow_run_id exists.
12. The target application URL/environment can be determined.
13. Required test data can be determined.

If any prerequisite fails:

- do not execute anything
- do not call Playwright MCP
- do not create ExecutionReport.md
- do not create BugReports.md
- record a blocked journal event
- stop

==================================================
INPUTS
==================================================

Primary input:

platform/tasks/<task-name>/test-design/TestCases.md

platform/tasks/<task-name>/context/ProjectContext.md

platform/tasks/<task-name>/exploration/ExplorationReport.md

TaskContext.md

Relevant PRD references where required for traceability.

Environment/build information.

Approved test data.

The approved TestCases.md is the execution contract.

==================================================
APPROVED TEST CASES ONLY
==================================================

Execute ONLY test cases that are present in the approved TestCases.md.

Do NOT:

- invent test cases
- add steps
- change expected results
- change preconditions
- change test data assumptions
- silently skip steps
- reinterpret the test case

If the test case is invalid or cannot be executed as written:

record:

Test Case Issue

and classify the execution result appropriately.

Do not silently repair it.

==================================================
TOOLING
==================================================

Use Playwright MCP for:

- browser interaction
- navigation
- entering test data
- clicking controls
- observing UI behavior
- screenshots
- accessibility snapshots
- relevant console output
- relevant network output
- evidence capture

Playwright MCP is the browser access tool.

It is NOT the automation framework.

Nothing created by this stage is automation code.

Do NOT generate:

- Playwright test scripts
- Selenium code
- SHAFT code
- Java code
- TestNG code
- automation framework code

Automation belongs to the next stage.

==================================================
ENVIRONMENT VERIFICATION
==================================================

Before executing the first test case:

record:

- environment
- application URL
- build/version identifier
- browser information if available
- execution date/time
- relevant environment configuration
- test data source

Do not fabricate any environment value.

If a required environment/build identifier cannot be determined:

record it as unavailable and explain why.

==================================================
TEST DATA VERIFICATION
==================================================

Before each test case:

1. Verify required test data exists.
2. Verify required preconditions can be established.
3. Do not expose secrets.
4. Do not invent credentials.
5. Use only approved/supplied test data.

If required test data is unavailable:

Status:

BLOCKED

Record:

- missing data
- affected test case
- why execution cannot proceed
- what would unblock it

==================================================
EXACT STEP EXECUTION
==================================================

Execute the steps exactly as written.

For every step:

1. Record the action.
2. Perform the action.
3. Observe the result.
4. Capture evidence when relevant.
5. Continue only if the execution state allows it.

If an execution deviation becomes necessary:

STOP and record:

- original step
- deviation
- reason
- consequence

Do NOT silently absorb deviations.

A deviation may invalidate the test result.

==================================================
EXPECTED RESULT
==================================================

The Expected Result MUST come directly from the approved test case.

Never rewrite it.

Never improve it.

Never infer a different expectation.

Example:

Approved Test Case:

Expected:
"User receives an error message."

Execution:

Expected:
"User receives an error message."

Actual:
"No error message is displayed."

Status:
FAIL

==================================================
ACTUAL RESULT
==================================================

Actual Result must describe only what was actually observed.

Do NOT write:

"The application should..."

Do NOT mix expected behavior into Actual Result.

Actual Result should contain:

- observed behavior
- exact text where relevant
- visible state
- navigation result
- relevant API/network behavior if observed
- relevant error details
- evidence reference

Never fabricate actual behavior.

==================================================
EXECUTION STATUS
==================================================

Every executed test case receives exactly one status:

PASS
FAIL
BLOCKED

==================================================
PASS
==================================================

Mark PASS only when:

1. The test case was actually executed.
2. All required steps were completed.
3. The actual result satisfies the approved Expected Result.
4. No blocking issue invalidated the result.

Never mark a case PASS because:

- it was expected to work
- the application usually works
- similar cases passed
- the AI assumes the result

A PASS requires real observation.

==================================================
FAIL
==================================================

Mark FAIL when:

- the test case was executed
- the observed behavior does not satisfy the approved Expected Result

Record:

- expected
- actual
- evidence
- failure reason
- affected step
- failure analysis

==================================================
BLOCKED
==================================================

Mark BLOCKED when the test case cannot be meaningfully executed because of an external or prerequisite condition.

Examples:

- environment unavailable
- required test data unavailable
- prerequisite system unavailable
- authentication unavailable
- required dependency unavailable
- application cannot be reached

Do not mark BLOCKED when the application itself executed the behavior and produced an incorrect result.

That is a FAIL.

==================================================
FAILURE ANALYSIS
==================================================

For every FAIL, determine the most supported cause category.

Use:

1. APPLICATION_DEFECT
2. TEST_CASE_DEFECT
3. TEST_DATA_PROBLEM
4. ENVIRONMENT_PROBLEM
5. DEPENDENCY_PROBLEM
6. UNKNOWN

Do not force a cause when evidence is insufficient.

Every failure analysis must contain:

- cause category
- reasoning
- supporting evidence
- confidence/limitations if necessary

==================================================
BUG VS TEST FAILURE
==================================================

A FAIL is NOT automatically a bug.

The stage must analyze whether the failure represents:

Application defect
OR
Test case defect
OR
Test data problem
OR
Environment problem
OR
Dependency problem
OR
Unknown

Only failures supported as application defects should produce bug reports.

Do not create bug reports for:

- test case defects
- missing test data
- environment failures
- dependency failures

unless the actual issue is itself a distinct application defect.

==================================================
DISTINCT DEFECT GROUPING
==================================================

Create ONE bug report per distinct defect/root cause.

If:

TC-001 FAIL
TC-004 FAIL
TC-009 FAIL

and all three failures are caused by the same underlying application defect:

create:

BUG-001

and associate:

TC-001
TC-004
TC-009

Do NOT create:

BUG-001
BUG-002
BUG-003

for the same root cause.

==================================================
BUG REPORT
==================================================

Create:

platform/tasks/<task-name>/bugs/BugReports.md

ONLY when supported application defects are found.

Each bug must contain:

## BUG-###

### Title

Clear concise defect title.

### Severity

Severity is assessed independently.

### Priority

Priority is assessed independently.

### Environment

Environment/build information.

### Preconditions

Required state before reproduction.

### Steps to Reproduce

Complete steps that allow a developer to reproduce the defect without external context.

### Expected Result

The expected result from the approved test case.

### Actual Result

The observed application behavior.

### Evidence

References to captured evidence.

### Affected Test Cases

All test cases failing because of this distinct defect.

### Root Cause Assessment

Only when supported by evidence.

Do not claim a technical root cause that was not established.

==================================================
SEVERITY
==================================================

Assess severity independently from priority.

Use the project's defined severity model if one exists.

Otherwise use:

Critical
High
Medium
Low

Do not invent severity based on personal preference.

Provide a severity rationale.

==================================================
PRIORITY
==================================================

Assess priority independently from severity.

Use documented business importance, risk, workflow impact, or project rules.

If priority cannot be established:

Not established

Provide the basis for the assigned priority.

Capture evidence for every FAIL and every bug where possible.

- screenshot

- network output

- relevant request/response information
- browser state
- relevant application output

Every evidence item must have:

- evidence ID

- related failure/bug if applicable
- MCP action that produced it
- file/reference
- timestamp when available
- redaction information

Never fabricate evidence.

If evidence cannot be captured:

record why.

We are continuing the existing AI-QA-Platform project.

## Objective: Audit the Current Architecture Before Continuing

I want to continue building this platform correctly, without duplicating existing work or introducing architectural inconsistencies.

Our intended direction is to support two automation frameworks:

1. Playwright Test + TypeScript.
2. SHAFT Engine + Selenium WebDriver + Java 21 + TestNG + Maven.

Both frameworks must eventually support automation code generation and execution. We also plan to create an attractive bilingual Arabic/English HTML experience that combines workflow observability with a concise educational comparison between the two frameworks.

However, DO NOT implement these new capabilities yet.

### 1. Inspect the Actual Repository

Read the current contents of:
- `CLAUDE.md`
- `README.md`
- `platform/workflows/default-qa-workflow.yaml`
- `platform/registry/skills.yaml`
- All current workflow and prompt schemas.
- All implemented skills, prompts, templates, and journal-event definitions.
- The task template and reporting architecture.
- `docs/architecture/ai-qa-execution-journal.md`, if present.
- All existing references to Playwright, Selenium, SHAFT, automation generation, HTML, and journal reporting.

Do not assume the documented architecture matches the actual implementation.

### 2. Assess the Workflow

Review the current stages and their dependencies.

The intended overall lifecycle is:

Task Creation → PRD Generation → Project Context → Exploration → Test Design → Test Review → Manual Execution → Automation Generation → Automation Execution → Self-Healing → Reporting.

Compare this intended lifecycle with the actual workflow configuration.

Identify missing stages, incorrect dependencies, missing skills, missing templates, inconsistent approval gates, and unclear responsibilities.

Do not change the workflow yet.

### 3. Assess the Automation Architecture

Determine whether the existing project can support both automation frameworks without conflicting configurations.

Identify:
- Existing framework assumptions.
- Conflicting references to Playwright and Selenium/SHAFT.
- Required framework-selection fields.
- How shared test case IDs and expected results should be preserved.
- How separate projects, dependencies, configurations, and execution reports should be organized.
- How execution results from both frameworks could be compared fairly.

Do not generate automation code or execute tests.

### 4. Assess the HTML Journal and Learning Center

Determine what actually exists today.

Distinguish clearly between:
- Implemented HTML pages.
- Existing data schemas and journal events.
- Templates that describe future HTML output.
- Documentation-only requirements.
- Missing implementation.

The eventual HTML experience must:
- Display the real workflow history.
- Show the exact prompt used at each stage, including version information.
- Link inputs, outputs, evidence, validation results, errors, fixes, and approvals.
- Distinguish planned, generated, validated, executed, failed, and blocked work.
- Include a concise bilingual learning section comparing Playwright and SHAFT/Selenium.
- Explain fundamental concepts and meaningful differences without explaining every code line.
- Show actual generated code separately from generic educational examples.

Do not build the HTML interface yet.

### 5. Validate Safely

Inspect existing validation scripts and available project checks.

Do not:
- Create a task.
- Access or test an application.
- Invoke Playwright MCP.
- Execute browser automation.
- Generate test cases or automation code.
- Modify existing files.
