ADR-001 (mandatory test review). Predates the task journal; outputs exist on disk.

Prompt text (exact; client wrapper tags removed)
We are continuing the existing AI-QA-Platform project. Do not rebuild the project from scratch.

## Objective
Record and implement the architecture decision that **Test Review is a mandatory workflow stage** before both Manual Execution and Automation Generation.

## 1. Audit Before Editing
Inspect the existing:
- `CLAUDE.md` and `README.md`
- `platform/workflows/default-qa-workflow.yaml`
- `platform/registry/skills.yaml`
- `platform/schemas/`
- Existing implemented Skills, their prompts, templates, and journal event formats
- Architecture documentation under `docs/architecture/`

Use the actual repository contents as the source of truth. Do not assume paths or schemas exist without checking them.

## 2. Mandatory Workflow
The intended lifecycle is:

Task Creation → PRD Generation → PRD Approval → Project Context → Project Context Approval → Exploration → Exploration Approval → Test Design → Test Review → Manual Execution → Automation Generation → Automation Execution → Self-Healing when needed → Reporting.

Preserve any existing approval gates and run-ID rules. Do not invent approval keys; inspect the current conventions and document any proposed changes.

## 3. Test Review Rules
- Test Review is mandatory for every task before execution can proceed.
- Manual Execution must depend on an approved Test Review.
- Automation Generation must also depend on the same approved Test Review.
- Neither stage may bypass Test Review, even if test cases already exist.
- Test Review must validate the test cases against the approved Project Context and Exploration findings.
- It must identify missing coverage, duplicates, unclear steps, invalid expected results, missing preconditions, and unsupported assumptions.
- Requested changes must be recorded and resolved before approval.
- Approval must be explicit, traceable, and tied to the exact reviewed artifact version or hash.
- Editing the reviewed artifact after approval must invalidate the approval.
- A blocked or unapproved review must prevent downstream execution and automation generation.
- Do not silently modify test cases during review. Record proposed changes and their reasons.

## 4. Dual Automation Frameworks
Preserve support for both:
- Playwright Test with TypeScript.
- SHAFT Engine + Selenium WebDriver + Java 21 + TestNG + Maven.

Do not generate or execute automation code in this task. Do not change the framework-selection architecture unless a verified conflict requires it; report such conflicts separately.

## 5. HTML Journal Requirements
Document this architecture decision so the future bilingual Arabic/English HTML Journal can show:
- The exact user decision and the prompt used to implement it.
- Files inspected, created, or modified.
- The workflow before and after the change.
- Validation checks and their actual results.
- Errors, fixes, assumptions, and unresolved questions.
- The effect of this decision on Manual Execution and Automation Generation.

Do not fabricate journal events or claim that the HTML page already exists. Follow the project's existing journal and prompt-versioning conventions.

## 6. Implementation Scope
Make only the minimum necessary changes to enforce the mandatory Test Review gate and keep the architecture documentation consistent.

Do not implement the orchestration runner, HTML renderer, or new Skills in this task.

Do not run tests against the real application. Use only relevant local structural, schema, and workflow validation.

## 7. Validation
After editing:
- Verify the workflow dependencies and ordering.
- Verify that Manual Execution cannot start without an approved Test Review.
- Verify that Automation Generation cannot start without the same approval.
- Verify stale approvals are rejected after the reviewed artifact changes.
- Verify existing schema, registry, prompt-versioning, and journal conventions remain consistent.
- Report the exact checks executed and their actual outcomes.

## 8. Final Response
Provide:
1. Files inspected.
2. Files created or modified.
3. The final workflow and its mandatory gates.
4. Validation results.
5. Remaining conflicts or decisions requiring my input.
6. A proposed next implementation step.

Stop after this task. Do not proceed to the next stage automatically.

No journal event records this prompt.

Task: Build Complete Project Context for OrangeHRM Directory Module

Exact text source: transcript ~/.claude/projects/c--Users-QC-User-Desktop-FinalAI/c19aceb8-92d5-47b3-ac42-f64ef63fb6bf.jsonl, line 3144 (uuid edadc587) · 8,675 characters

Prompt text (exact; client wrapper tags removed)
# Task: Build Complete Project Context for OrangeHRM Directory Module

Act as a **Senior QA Engineer, Business Analyst, and UI Exploration Specialist**.

Your task is to deeply analyze and document the **OrangeHRM Directory Module** so that this documentation becomes the reliable Project Context for all upcoming QA activities.

## 1. Application Under Test

- Application: OrangeHRM
- URL: https://opensource-demo.orangehrmlive.com/web/index.php/directory/viewDirectory
- Module: Directory
- Scope: UI testing only.
- API testing is explicitly excluded.
- Use the existing project instructions, skills, agents, and documentation standards already available in this repository.
- Do not create unnecessary skills, agents, frameworks, or architecture.

## 2. Main Objective

Explore the Directory module and understand its functionality, UI components, user interactions, business rules, search behavior, displayed data, validation, and possible error states.

The resulting documentation must allow another QA engineer or AI agent to understand the module without having to rediscover its basic functionality.

**Important:** Use the actual running application as the primary source of truth. Do not assume functionality exists just because it is common in HR systems.

## 3. Explore the Module

Use the available browser exploration tools, including Playwright MCP if configured, to inspect the real UI.

First, inspect the current page and identify the available controls.

### A. Directory Page Layout

Document:

- Page title and purpose.
- Navigation path to the Directory module.
- Sidebar navigation and the selected Directory item.
- Search/filter panel.
- Results count.
- Employee cards or result items.
- Employee names and profile images.
- Scrolling behavior and page layout.
- Loading indicators, empty states, errors, and other states if observed.
- Any pagination or additional result-loading mechanism, if present.

### B. Search and Filter Controls

Inspect each control individually.

**Employee Name**
- Input type and placeholder.
- Whether it supports autocomplete or suggestions.
- Whether partial names and full names work.
- Whether it is case-sensitive.
- How the application handles leading/trailing spaces.
- What happens when the input matches no employee.
- What happens when the field is empty.

**Job Title**
- Available options.
- Default selection.
- How the options are populated.
- Whether the selected value can be combined with other filters.
- Search results for selected values.

**Location**
- Available options.
- Default selection.
- How the options are populated.
- Whether the selected value can be combined with other filters.
- Search results for selected values.

**Search button**
- What happens when it is clicked.
- Whether all entered filters are applied together.
- Whether results update immediately or after submission.
- Whether the results count changes.
- How the application behaves when no results match.

**Reset button**
- Which fields are cleared or restored.
- Whether results return to the default state.
- Whether the results count is restored.
- Whether reset works when some or all filters are populated.

Also inspect the filter panel expand/collapse control if available.

### C. Employee Results

Inspect employee result cards and document:

- Information displayed on each card.
- Employee name formatting.
- Profile image or placeholder behavior.
- Whether clicking a card or employee name opens another page or dialog.
- Any additional details revealed by interacting with a card.
- What happens when an employee has a missing or unusual value, if observable.
- How the UI behaves when the number of results is large.
- Scrolling behavior within the results area.

Do not assume that cards are clickable. Verify their behavior.

### D. Search Behavior and Combinations

Explore the supported combinations of:

1. Employee Name only.
2. Job Title only.
3. Location only.
4. Employee Name + Job Title.
5. Employee Name + Location.
6. Job Title + Location.
7. All three filters together.
8. No filters.
9. A value that returns no results.
10. Clearing or resetting previously applied filters.

Use a small number of representative searches first. Expand the exploration where needed to determine the actual behavior.

Do not claim that a scenario passed or failed unless it was actually executed and observed.

### E. UI States and Edge Cases

Investigate where applicable:

- Empty input.
- Partial and exact employee names.
- Invalid or nonexistent employee names.
- Leading and trailing spaces.
- Different letter casing.
- Switching between filter values.
- Repeated Search clicks.
- Reset after a successful search.
- Reset after a zero-result search.
- Long employee names.
- Missing profile images.
- Browser refresh and navigation behavior.

Only report states that you can observe or reasonably verify. Clearly label anything that could not be tested.

## 4. Data and Evidence

For every important observation:

- Record the observed behavior.
- Record the steps used to observe it.
- Capture screenshots when supported and useful.
- Record the relevant page URL.
- Distinguish observed facts from assumptions and unanswered questions.
- Do not expose passwords, tokens, cookies, or other sensitive authentication data in documentation.

Do not invent employee information, dropdown options, result counts, or business rules.

## 5. Documentation to Create

First inspect the existing repository structure and reuse its established locations and formats.

Create or update the following documentation as appropriate:

### `Directory-Module-Overview.md`
Include:
- Module purpose.
- Navigation and page structure.
- Main features.
- Search/filter controls.
- Results display.
- User interactions.
- Dependencies and observed behavior.
- Known limitations and open questions.

### `Directory-Functional-Analysis.md`
Include:
- Detailed behavior of each control.
- Input and selection behavior.
- Search and reset logic.
- Filter combinations.
- Results behavior.
- UI states and edge cases.
- Observed rules versus assumptions.

### `Directory-UI-Inventory.md`
Create a structured inventory of:
- UI element name.
- Element type.
- Location on the page.
- User action.
- Observed response.
- Locator candidates, if inspected.
- Notes or uncertainties.

Do not invent locators. If locator inspection is supported, prefer IDs and XPath for this project's UI automation conventions.

### `Directory-Exploration-Report.md`
Include:
- Exploration date.
- Application URL.
- Exploration scope.
- Areas explored.
- Scenarios actually performed.
- Results and evidence.
- Unverified scenarios.
- Bugs or suspicious behavior observed.
- Remaining questions.

If the repository already has equivalent documents or a prescribed documentation structure, update or reuse those instead of creating duplicate files.

## 6. Project Context Requirements

The resulting Project Context must be useful for future stages:

- Test Design.
- Test Case Review.
- Manual Test Execution.
- UI Automation Generation.
- Automation Execution.
- Defect Reporting.

It must clearly explain what the Directory module does, how its controls behave, what data is displayed, and which behaviors remain uncertain.

Do not generate the final test cases yet. This stage is for understanding and documenting the module.

Do not execute a full test suite. Limited exploratory interactions are allowed to establish the module's actual behavior.

Do not perform API testing.

## 7. Quality and Accuracy Rules

1. Actual application behavior takes priority over assumptions.
2. Never invent functionality, business rules, test results, or evidence.
3. Clearly distinguish `Observed`, `Inferred`, and `Not Verified`.
4. If the application or browser is unavailable, report the blocker instead of pretending exploration succeeded.
5. Reuse the existing project structure and instructions.
6. Keep the documentation clear, structured, and free of unnecessary repetition.
7. Record the work in the existing project journal according to the repository's workflow.
8. Preserve previous files and user decisions; do not overwrite unrelated work.

## 8. Final Response

When finished, provide a concise summary containing:

- Files created or updated and their paths.
- Areas of the Directory module actually explored.
- Important confirmed behaviors.
- Any bugs discovered, without claiming unverified defects.
- Areas not explored and the reasons.
- Whether the Project Context is ready for the next stage.

**Stop after completing the documentation and journal updates. Do not proceed to Test Design or execution until explicitly instructed.**

- Evidence
- Reproducibility status

Exact text source: transcript ~/.claude/projects/c--Users-QC-User-Desktop-FinalAI-AI-QA-Platform/81f4a30e-2971-4ea8-85d6-0f96355d282b.jsonl, line 13644 (uuid 0c131d82) · 5,871 characters

Prompt text (exact; client wrapper tags removed)
# Refactor SHAFT Automation to Match My Existing My Bookings Framework

I want the SHAFT implementation of TC-054 to follow the same coding style, architecture, and conventions used in my existing My Bookings automation project.

**Important:** Do not write a generic Java automation script. Follow the established project pattern described below.

## 1. Test Class Structure — TestNG

The test class must use TestNG annotations and organize execution into lifecycle methods:

- `@BeforeClass`: Initialize test data, set up the browser, initialize the SHAFT driver, configure the browser, navigate to the application, and perform login when appropriate.
- `@Test`: Execute the test scenario by calling Page Object methods and performing assertions.
- `@AfterClass`: Close the browser and clean up resources, following the existing project conventions.

Use `org.testng.annotations.Test`, `BeforeClass`, `AfterClass`, and TestNG assertions where appropriate.

Follow the My Bookings pattern of reusing one browser session for the class when appropriate, rather than unnecessarily creating a new browser or logging in for every individual step.

## 2. SHAFT Driver and Test Data

Use the existing SHAFT APIs and project utilities:

- Browser driver: `SHAFT.GUI.WebDriver`
- Test data: `SHAFT.TestData.JSON`
- Test data access: follow the existing `DataUtils.get(...)` convention where applicable.
- Browser setup: follow the existing `CommonMethod` utilities and browser configuration conventions when compatible with the target project.

Keep credentials and environment-specific configuration outside the test source. Never hard-code credentials or expose them in logs or reports.

Use JSON for configurable test data where appropriate, while keeping employee names, result counts, and other dynamic application data runtime-driven.

## 3. Page Object Model — Separate Responsibilities

Do not put all Selenium interactions, locators, navigation, and assertions inside the test class.

Separate the implementation into dedicated Page Objects:

**Test Class**
- Controls the scenario's execution flow.
- Calls Page Object methods in the correct order.
- Performs the test assertions.
- Keeps the test readable and understandable.

**Page Objects**
- Own their relevant locators and UI interactions.
- Expose meaningful public methods.
- Handle page-specific navigation and state checks.
- Return `this` or the appropriate Page Object when method chaining is useful.

Follow the separation demonstrated by `MyBookings_TC.java`, `MyBookings_Page.java`, and `BookingDetails_Page.java`.

## 4. Readable Method Chaining

Use descriptive, chainable methods so the test reads like a sequence of business actions.

For example, the intended style is:

```java
directoryPage
    .navigateToDirectory()
    .selectJobTitle(jobTitle)
    .selectLocation(location)
    .clickSearch()
    .waitUntilResultsUpdated();
```

The example illustrates the desired style; adapt method names and return types to the actual implementation.

Avoid placing long sequences of low-level `findElement`, `click`, `sendKeys`, or XPath operations directly inside the test method. Keep those details inside the relevant Page Objects.

## 5. Locators, Waits, and Assertions

- Keep locators inside Page Objects.
- Use stable, verified locators.
- Follow the existing project's locator conventions where appropriate.
- Use explicit, condition-based waits for dynamic UI state changes.
- Do not use fixed sleeps.
- Keep assertions in the test class unless an existing project convention provides a clear reason to encapsulate a page-level validation.
- Use clear assertion messages identifying the expected and actual behavior.
- Preserve all five approved TC-054 checks and their original expected results.

## 6. Preserve TC-054 Behavior

Refactor the code structure without changing the approved test scenario.

The test must still:

1. Navigate to the Directory UI and authenticate as required.
2. Dynamically obtain the Job Title and Location pair according to the approved DATA-012 behavior.
3. Search using that pair and capture the filtered count `k`.
4. Open and close the employee details panel.
5. Click Reset and wait for the results to update.
6. Capture the reset count `N` and verify the approved condition `N > k`.

Do not hard-code employee names, Job Titles, Locations, or result counts. Do not weaken, remove, or rewrite any approved assertion to make the test pass.

## 7. Reference Implementation

Use these existing files as the architectural reference:

- `MyBookings_TC.java` — test lifecycle, TestNG annotations, driver initialization, test-data setup, and test execution flow.
- `MyBookings_Page.java` — Page Object structure, navigation methods, locators, reusable UI actions, waits, and method chaining.
- `BookingDetails_Page.java` — another example of page-specific locators, helper methods, readable encapsulation, and dynamic UI handling.

Inspect the actual files before making changes. Reuse existing utilities and conventions when compatible, but do not blindly copy My Bookings-specific logic into OrangeHRM.

## 8. Validation and Final Response

After refactoring:

- Compile the Maven project.
- Execute TC-054 and verify the actual result.
- Preserve execution evidence.
- Confirm the approved test steps and assertions are unchanged.
- List the exact files modified.
- Explain how the resulting test class and Page Objects follow the My Bookings architecture.

Do not modify the Playwright implementation, approved upstream artifacts, or unrelated project files. Do not perform Git operations or create unrelated bug reports.

**Start by inspecting the existing My Bookings implementation and the current TC-054 SHAFT implementation. Then refactor only what is necessary to match the established coding style.**

Prompt text (exact; client wrapper tags removed)
Simplify the SHAFT TC-054 automation code. I want basic, readable Java automation using:

- Java 21
- Selenium WebDriver through SHAFT Engine
- TestNG
- Maven
- Page Object Model

Follow the simple coding style of my My Bookings project, but prioritize readability and simplicity over unnecessary abstractions.

### Coding Rules

1. Keep the TestNG class straightforward, using `@BeforeClass`, `@Test`, and `@AfterClass`.
2. Keep the test method readable, with clear, sequential calls to Page Object methods.
3. Use `SHAFT.GUI.WebDriver` and the existing project setup utilities.
4. Keep locators and basic browser interactions inside Page Objects.
5. Use simple method chaining only where it improves readability.
6. Avoid unnecessary `try/catch`, nested `if/else`, complex helper methods, custom exception classes, excessive abstractions, and complicated logging mechanisms.
7. Do not create extra classes or utilities unless they are genuinely needed.
8. Use normal TestNG `SoftAssert` and `assertAll()` for the approved checks.
9. Use straightforward waits with the project's standard 30-second timeout. No fixed sleeps.
10. Keep test data dynamic and credentials in environment variables. Do not hard-code names, counts, or secrets.
11. Keep screenshot and report handling simple, using existing SHAFT/TestNG capabilities wherever possible.
12. Do not use JavaScript helpers or complicated custom frameworks when a basic Java/Selenium/SHAFT solution is sufficient.

### Preserve the Test

Keep the approved TC-054 steps, all five checks, DATA-012 dynamic Job Title and Location selection, employee details open/close flow, Reset action, and the `N > k` assertion unchanged.

### Before Editing

Inspect the current code and identify unnecessary complexity. Then simplify only the SHAFT implementation. Do not change Playwright or unrelated files.

Compile and execute TC-054 after the refactoring, and verify the actual result. Preserve existing evidence and approved artifacts. Do not perform Git operations.

At the end, show me the simplified test class and Page Objects, explain the code in simple terms, list changed files, and report the actual test result.

**Priority: simple, readable, maintainable code over advanced abstractions or overengineering.**

# Create the Complete AI Prompt & Output History HTML

Project: AI-QA-Platform
Task scope: OrangeHRM UI testing project.

## Objective
Create one standalone HTML document that presents the actual prompt history used throughout the project and the corresponding outputs, validations, and execution results.

Output path:
`platform/tasks/orangehrm-ui-api/reports/AIPromptOutputHistory.html`

## Sources to inspect

- `.claude/` prompts, skills, agents, and configuration files

- `journal/execution-log.json`
- `automation-execution/execution-metrics.json`
- PRD, exploration, test design and review artifacts
- Manual execution report and bug reports
- Automation generation, execution and self-healing records

- Existing output artifacts and validation reports

## Required content

1. Sequence number and milestone.

- Automation execution

# Prompt — PRD Generation

> Versioning rule: any change to the text of this file requires a
> `prompt_version` bump. The journal identifies the prompt used for each run by
> `prompt_id` + `prompt_version` and stores one verbatim snapshot of each version,
> with its SHA-256, in the task's `journal/prompts.json`.

## Purpose

Convert the task's supplied information into a structured, traceable **Product
Requirements Document** that the Project Context stage can consume. The PRD
records **documented intent**. It is not an exploration report, test case
document, bug report, automation plan, or execution report.

## Expected Inputs

| Input | Location |
|---|---|
| Task identity (Task ID, Name, Type, Project Name) | `{{task_path}}/context/TaskContext.md` § Identity |
| Project UI / API URL (if provided) | `{{task_path}}/context/TaskContext.md` § Project Entry Points |
| Task description / user story, acceptance criteria, scope, out of scope, additional information (verbatim intake) | `{{task_path}}/context/TaskContext.md` § Task Intake |
| Supporting documents (local only) | Paths listed in the intake or supplied by the user |
| Information supplied by the user in the request | The user's message, recorded verbatim in the journal |
| workflow_run_id | `{{workflow_run_id}}`, from `journal/workflow-run.json` (owned by orchestration) |

## Source-of-Truth Rules

Use sources in this priority order:

1. Explicit user or task requirements
2. Explicit acceptance criteria
3. Explicit project information
4. Verified source information from the provided project URL (see URL rules)
5. Clearly labeled assumptions
6. Unknown, recorded as a GAP or Open Question

- **Never silently fill gaps.** If a statement is not supported by a source,
  mark it `Unknown` or raise an Open Question.
- **Never invent** business rules, roles, permissions, workflows, API behavior,
  validation rules, database behavior, UI behavior, error messages, expected
  results, acceptance criteria, owners, or non-functional values.
- **Never silently resolve ambiguity or conflict.** If two sources disagree,
  record a GAP citing both and do not pick one.
- An assumption never becomes a requirement. Assumptions appear only in §17.

### URL rules

- Check that each provided URL is present and well-formed, and record it as a
  source (`SRC-###`).
- You may perform **at most one plain, non-browser HTTP request per provided
  entry URL**. From it, establish only:
  - (a) whether the URL is reachable, with its HTTP status, and
  - (b) how the resource identifies itself (e.g. its page or document title).
- Do not use a browser or Playwright MCP. Do not navigate, follow links, log
  in, submit forms, inspect the DOM or network traffic, discover or call API
  endpoints, or record UI elements, fields, messages or credentials seen in the
  response. All of that belongs to **Exploration**.
- If a URL is inaccessible, or no suitable tool is available, record the
  limitation and do not fabricate information. Continue with the supplied
  information if it is sufficient. Record what is missing as a GAP or Open
  Question.

## Generation Instructions

1. Read `TaskContext.md` completely, then every supporting document and the
   user-supplied information.
2. Register every source as `SRC-001`, `SRC-002`, … in §2. Cite user-supplied
   chat information as `User message (journal EVT-NNNN)`.
3. Apply the URL rules.
4. Write each section of the PRD structure below. For each statement, cite its
   `SRC-###` (plus section or quote where useful).
5. Split compound statements so each FR and BR is a single, independently
   understandable, traceable and, where possible, testable statement.
6. Preserve supplied acceptance criteria **verbatim** (`AC-###`, keeping any
   original labels). If none were supplied, write exactly:
   `Acceptance criteria were not explicitly provided.`
7. Where a section has no supported content, write the section's required "not
   provided" wording. Never leave a section out.
8. Build §19 Traceability: Source → Requirement → Business Rule → Acceptance
   Criterion.
9. Build §20 QA Preparation Notes: what later stages must explore or validate.
   Never claim that anything has been observed.
10. Write the result to `{{task_path}}/prd/PRD.md`, following
    `.claude/skills/prd-generation/PRD.template.md`.

## PRD Structure

`# Product Requirements Document`, followed by these sections, in order:

1. **Document Information**: PRD ID (`PRD-<task-id>`), Task ID, Task Name,
   Project Name, Task Type, Version, Status, workflow_run_id, Created timestamp,
   Last updated timestamp, and revision history. Timestamps come from the system
   clock and the run ID from the run context. Never fabricate them.
2. **Source Information**: the `SRC-###` register (user or task requirement, UI
   URL, API URL, supporting documents, other sources), with access status for
   URLs.
3. **Problem Statement**: only as described. Do not expand it.
4. **Goal**: the user's stated intent. Do not invent business goals.
5. **User Story**: As a… / I want… / So that…, or `Not provided.`
6. **Scope**: In Scope (documented only), Out of Scope (explicit exclusions
   only), Scope Not Defined.
7. **Actors / Roles**: role, responsibility, and permissions only if explicitly
   stated.
8. **Functional Requirements**: `FR-###`, each with *Requirement* and *Source*.
9. **Business Rules**: `BR-###`, each independently verifiable, with source.
10. **Acceptance Criteria**: verbatim `AC-###`, or the not-provided statement.
11. **Data Requirements**: `DR-###`, known data requirements only. No test data.
12. **UI Requirements**: `UIR-###`, only if explicitly supported. No selectors,
    layouts, field behavior, messages or components unless stated.
13. **API Requirements**: `APIR-###`, only if explicitly supported. No invented
    endpoints, schemas, authentication or status codes. If only an API URL is
    known, record the entry point and mark detailed behavior as
    `Requires exploration`.
14. **Non-Functional Requirements**: `NFR-###` for performance, security,
    accessibility, reliability, compatibility, availability or other attributes
    as provided, or `Not explicitly provided.`
15. **Dependencies**: `DEP-###` for internal modules, external systems, APIs,
    authentication, data, configuration, or other tasks, where supported.
16. **Constraints**: `CON-###` for explicit technology, business, scope,
    environment or data constraints.
17. **Assumptions**: `AS-###` with description, reason, and source or context.
18. **Gaps and Open Questions**: `GAP-###` / `OQ-###` with description,
    related requirement, impact if known, and owner (`not stated` unless
    explicitly provided).
19. **Traceability**: Source → Requirement → Business Rule → Acceptance
    Criterion. Every FR has a source.
20. **QA Preparation Notes**: areas requiring exploration, UI areas requiring
    validation, API areas requiring validation, business rules requiring
    validation, data requiring validation, and unknown behavior requiring
    investigation. This is preparation only.

## Quality Rules

- The PRD is structured, traceable, testable, evidence-based, reusable,
  unambiguous where possible, and explicit about uncertainty.
- One statement per FR / BR / AC. Keep wording neutral, and quote sources where
  precision matters.
- Only requirement content. No test cases, test data, exploration findings, bug
  reports, automation or execution content.
- Nothing about the application beyond what the sources state and what the URL
  rules allow.

## Stop Conditions

- **After writing PRD.md: STOP.** Do not create ProjectContext.md, explore the
  application, generate test cases, execute tests, generate automation or bugs,
  or produce reports.
- **Before writing anything, STOP and report failure** if:
  - the task folder is missing,
  - the target is the template,
  - TaskContext.md is missing,
  - there is no active workflow run,
  - there is no requirement input at all,
  - PRD.md already exists without a journaled `prd-generation` origin (never
    overwrite a human-authored PRD).

## Approval Requirements

- Progression to Project Context requires the exact standalone key:

  ```
  PRD_APPROVED
  ```

- "approved", "looks good", "okay", "continue", "proceed", "LGTM", any other
  text containing the key, the key mixed with other text, silence, and another
  stage's key are **not** approval.
- If changes are requested, update the **same** `PRD.md` (never `PRD-v2.md` or
  `PRD-final.md`), increment the PRD minor version, record the reason and the
  changed sections, present the revision, and STOP again.

# Prompt — Project Context

> Versioning rule: any change to the text of this file requires a
> `prompt_version` bump. The journal identifies the prompt used for each run by
> `prompt_id` + `prompt_version` and stores one snapshot of each version in the
> task's `journal/prompts.json`. The snapshot is compared by SHA-256.

## Purpose

Establish what the task **is**, as documented by its PRD: its functional areas,
scope and boundaries, roles, business rules, expected behaviors, dependencies,
assumptions, risks, gaps and open questions. Every item must trace back to its
source. The output becomes the scope source of truth for every later QA stage of
this task once a human approves it.

## Expected Inputs

| Input | Location | Required |
|---|---|---|
| Task PRD | `{{prd_path}}` (normally `{{task_path}}/prd/PRD.md`) | Yes |
| Task identity metadata | `{{task_path}}/context/TaskContext.md` § Identity | Identity fields only (Section 1) |
| Shared knowledge-base material | `platform/knowledge-base/…`, only files the PRD references or depends on | When referenced |
| Supporting material | Local files the PRD explicitly references | When referenced |

## Instructions

You are performing the **Project Context** stage for task `{{task_name}}`.

1. Confirm the PRD was approved with `PRD_APPROVED` and has not changed since.
   Then read the task PRD **completely** before proposing anything.
2. Read only the knowledge-base and supporting material that the PRD references
   or depends on. If referenced material is not available locally, do not fetch
   or guess it. Record it as a GAP.
3. Do **not** consult the live application: no browser, no Playwright MCP, no API
   calls, and no fetching the application's URLs. The PRD is the source of
   documented intent. Observed behavior belongs to the Exploration stage.
4. Perform a **Scope Decomposition**. Split the task into distinct functional
   areas or flows. For each one, give its name, a description, what it contains,
   and why it is distinct.
5. Define **Task Scope**:
   - In scope: only what the PRD documents.
   - Explicitly out of scope: only what the PRD explicitly excludes. Never infer
     exclusions.
   - Scope boundaries: where this task meets other functionality.
6. Identify **Roles and Permissions**: allowed and restricted actions per role,
   as supported by the PRD.
7. Extract **Business Rules** as numbered (`BR-001`, …), independently verifiable
   statements, one assertion per rule, each with its PRD source.
8. Extract **Expected Behaviors** (`EB-001`, …) that the PRD explicitly states,
   each with its PRD source.
9. Identify **Dependencies** (other tasks, modules, external systems, APIs, data,
   business), only where documentation supports them.
10. List **Assumptions** (`AS-001`, …). Mark each one `Confirmed` (stated in a
    source) or `Unconfirmed`. Never present an assumption as a fact, and never
    turn one into a requirement.
11. Identify **Risks** (`RK-001`, …). Mark each one's basis as `Documented`
    (stated in a source) or `Derived` (QA reasoning). Say why it matters, which
    requirement or area it relates to, and cite the source.
12. Identify **Gaps** (`GAP-001`, …: missing, unclear, contradictory or untestable
    requirements) and **Open Questions** (`OQ-001`, …: specific questions). Name
    an owner **only** if a source or the human explicitly states one. Otherwise
    write `not stated`.
13. Never invent requirements. Never silently resolve an unclear business rule.
    If the PRD does not state something, record it as a GAP or OPEN QUESTION.
14. Build **Traceability**: PRD Section → Requirement → Business Rule / Expected
    Behavior → Scope Area. Use the PRD's own requirement IDs where they exist,
    always qualified as `PRD <ID>` (e.g. `PRD FR-003`) so they are never
    confused with this artifact's own IDs.
    Otherwise cite the section plus a short quote. List any PRD section that
    produced no item.
15. Prepare **QA Scope Preparation** for downstream stages: the areas, UI areas,
    API areas, business rules and data that require exploration or validation,
    by reference to the IDs above. This is preparation only. Do not explore.
16. Write the result to `{{task_path}}/context/ProjectContext.md` using the
    structure in `.claude/skills/project-context/ProjectContext.template.md`. If
    the file already exists, update it in place.

## Expected Outputs

- Exactly one artifact: `{{task_path}}/context/ProjectContext.md`, containing
  sections 1–12 (Task Information, Scope Decomposition, Task Scope, Roles and
  Permissions, Business Rules, Expected Behaviors, Dependencies, Assumptions,
  Risks, Gaps and Open Questions, Traceability, QA Scope Preparation).
- A short review summary to the human: artifact path, counts per section, the
  most important gaps and open questions, and the required approval key.

## Stop Conditions

- **After writing ProjectContext.md: STOP.** Do not explore, design tests,
  execute, generate or prepare automation, draft any later stage, or modify the
  PRD.
- **Before writing anything, STOP and report failure** if:
  - the task folder does not exist,
  - the target is the task template,
  - the PRD is missing, empty or an unfilled placeholder,
  - the PRD is not approved with `PRD_APPROVED`, or changed after approval,
  - there is no active workflow run (`workflow_run_id` is never created here),
  - the PRD delegates its requirements entirely to material that is unavailable.

## Approval Requirements

- Progression to the next stage requires the literal key, sent on its own:

  ```
  PROJECT_CONTEXT_APPROVED
  ```

- "looks good", "approved", "continue", "okay", "proceed", "LGTM", silence, and
  another stage's key are **not** approval.
- If changes are requested, update the **same** `ProjectContext.md` (never
  `-v2` or `-final`), present the revision, and STOP again.

# Prompt — Exploration

> Versioning rule: any change to the text of this file requires a
> `prompt_version` bump. The journal identifies the prompt used for each run by
> `prompt_id` + `prompt_version`. One verbatim snapshot of each version, with its
> SHA-256, is stored in the task's `journal/prompts.json`.

## Purpose

Learn how the application **actually behaves** before any test case is written.
Record every place where reality and the approved Project Context agree or
disagree. Test design that skips this step encodes assumptions instead of
behavior.

```
approved ProjectContext.md + live application → expected vs actual → ExplorationReport.md → HUMAN REVIEW → EXPLORATION_APPROVED
```

## Expected Inputs

| Input | Location | Use |
|---|---|---|
| Approved Project Context | `{{task_path}}/context/ProjectContext.md` | **Expected-behavior baseline** |
| PRD | `{{task_path}}/prd/PRD.md` | Reference only: IDs, sources, entry points |
| Task identity and entry points | `{{task_path}}/context/TaskContext.md` | Task ID, name, project, environment, URLs |
| Live application | UI: `{{ui_url}}` · API: `{{api_url}}` | **Actual behavior**, via Playwright MCP |
| workflow_run_id | `{{workflow_run_id}}` (orchestration-owned, read only) | Journal and report header |

## Prerequisites

Do not explore, and do not open a browser, unless **all** of these hold:

- the task, TaskContext.md, PRD.md and ProjectContext.md exist,
- ProjectContext.md was approved with `PROJECT_CONTEXT_APPROVED`, and that
  approval is current (the file is unchanged since approval, and the PRD approval
  is also current),
- an active `workflow_run_id` exists.

Otherwise, stop and report which prerequisite is missing.

## ProjectContext Rules

- The approved ProjectContext is the **only** expectation baseline. Use its
  in-scope and out-of-scope areas, roles and permissions, business rules
  (`PC BR-###`), expected behaviors (`PC EB-###`), dependencies, risks, gaps,
  open questions and §12 QA Scope Preparation.
- Never rewrite ProjectContext to match the application. Never assume either the
  application or the documentation is correct. Record the difference.
- Exploration never closes a ProjectContext GAP or OQ. It records what was
  observed about it, for a human to judge.

## Exploration Instructions

1. **Plan before touching the browser.** For each area in ProjectContext §12,
   and each in-scope `SA-###`, list:
   - the expected behaviors and rules to compare,
   - the UI or API flow to follow,
   - the data needed.

   This plan is §2 of the report.
2. For each planned area:
   1. identify the expected behavior,
   2. identify the flow,
   3. explore it,
   4. record the actual behavior,
   5. compare expected with actual,
   6. classify,
   7. capture evidence where required.
3. Inspect, where applicable:
   - **fields**: type, required or optional, format, limits, defaults, visible
     and validation behavior
   - **dropdowns**: actual options, ordering, default and empty state, dynamic
     loading where observable
   - **validations**: when they fire, the exact message text, client or server
     side where observable, behavior after invalid input and after correction
   - **dependencies**: fields or sections that change, appear, disappear,
     enable or disable, and links between screens
   - **workflows**: the actual end-to-end path, states, transitions,
     navigation, success and failure behavior
   - **relevant APIs**: only when API behavior is in scope, or needed to
     understand an in-scope UI flow
4. Stay in scope. Follow an out-of-scope area only when it is a dependency of
   an in-scope flow, and record why. Never click through the application at
   random.

## Playwright MCP Rules

- Use the `playwright` MCP server for **all** browser access. It is a
  browser-access and exploration tool, **not** the automation framework.
- Allowed: open the application, navigate, interact with in-scope UI, and take
  accessibility snapshots and screenshots. Read-only DOM inspection of element
  attributes is allowed. So is observing network requests and console messages
  produced by in-scope actions.
- Forbidden:
  - writing automation code of any kind (Playwright tests, Selenium, SHAFT,
    TestNG, Java),
  - using page scripts to alter state, bypass validation or perform actions a
    user could not perform,
  - exploring outside scope without a recorded reason.
- **Data-changing actions:**
  - Only perform them when needed to observe an in-scope flow, and record each
    one as data-changing.
  - Destructive, irreversible or bulk actions, and actions on data not created
    during this exploration, require explicit human confirmation first.
- **Credentials:**
  - Use only credentials supplied by the task inputs or the human.
  - If the application itself displays credentials, record that as an
    observation and ask before using them.
  - Never write passwords, tokens or cookies into the report or journal. Redact
    them.

## Expected vs Actual Rules

Every comparison has exactly this shape:

```
Expected:       <documented expectation from ProjectContext — never an observation>
Actual:         <observed application behavior — never an expectation>
Classification: MATCH | FINDING | GAP | QUESTION | POTENTIAL BUG
Evidence:       EVD-### (or "not required — MATCH", or reason not captured)
Source:         PC BR-### / PC EB-### / PC SA-### (or "none — ProjectContext silent")
```

If ProjectContext is silent, Expected is `Not documented (ProjectContext silent)`.
Never fill Expected with what the application does.

## Classification Rules

Every observation gets **exactly one** classification. Apply these steps in order:

1. **POTENTIAL BUG**: objective malfunction signals, whether documented or not.
   For example: an unhandled error, crash, HTTP 5xx, broken or blank page, lost or
   corrupted data, or exposure of sensitive data. This is suspected, never
   confirmed.
2. **MATCH / FINDING**: a clear documented expectation exists. MATCH if actual
   equals expected. FINDING if it differs. A difference is a FINDING, **not** a
   bug, because the documentation may be the thing that is wrong.
3. **QUESTION**: the expectation exists but is ambiguous or contradictory, or
   judging the behavior needs a human or business decision.
4. **GAP**: ProjectContext is silent about the observed behavior. Do not
   decide what the requirement should be.

Never upgrade a POTENTIAL BUG to a confirmed bug. Never turn an observation
into a requirement or business rule.

## Evidence Rules

- Capture evidence for **every non-MATCH** observation. For a MATCH, evidence is
  optional.
- Evidence types:
  - screenshot
  - accessibility snapshot
  - element attributes
  - URL
  - page title
  - network or API request/response
  - console messages
  - browser state
- Each item gets an `EVD-###` ID and records:
  - type
  - the source MCP action
  - related observation(s)
  - file path in `{{task_path}}/exploration/evidence/`
  - system timestamp
  - SHA-256
- Store evidence files under `{{task_path}}/exploration/evidence/`. Move files
  the MCP server wrote elsewhere (e.g. `.playwright-mcp/`) into that folder.
- Save text evidence verbatim, redacting only secrets and marking each
  redaction.
- If evidence cannot be captured, record an evidence item with
  `capture_status: not_captured` and the real reason. Never fabricate evidence.

## Locator Rules

- For relevant elements, record `LOC-###` with:
  - element description and page,
  - the stable `id`, if present,
  - a meaningful attribute usable in XPath (attribute name and observed value),
  - locator notes,
  - stability observations (e.g. generated or dynamic id, changes on reload,
    position-dependent),
  - related observation and evidence.
- Record **observed attribute values only**. Never record Playwright locator
  syntax (`getByRole`, `getByText`, `getByLabel`, `getByTestId`), Playwright MCP
  snapshot refs (e.g. `ref=e12`, which are session-scoped), or any Selenium,
  SHAFT or TestNG code. The automation stage decides the implementation. It uses
  an ID/XPath preference.

## Output Structure

Write `{{task_path}}/exploration/ExplorationReport.md` following
`.claude/skills/exploration/ExplorationReport.template.md`, with these sections
in this order:

1. Exploration Information
2. Exploration Scope (with the exploration plan)
3. Environment
4. Exploration Method
5. Observations
6. MATCHES
7. FINDINGS
8. GAPS
9. QUESTIONS
10. POTENTIAL BUGS
11. Candidate Locators
12. Dependencies Discovered
13. Coverage Limits
14. Exploration Summary

§14 gives counts and coverage limits only. It gives **no** overall quality
verdict.

## Hard-Stop Rules

After ExplorationReport.md is written: **STOP** and present it for review. Do
not:

- generate or design test cases,
- write automation,
- execute tests,
- create bug reports,
- modify ProjectContext or the PRD,
- continue to Test Design or automation.

## Approval Rules

- The only progression key is the exact, case-sensitive, standalone value:

  ```
  EXPLORATION_APPROVED
  ```

- These are **not** approval: `approved`, `looks good`, `continue`, `proceed`,
  `LGTM`, `EXPLORATION_APPROVED please continue`, any message containing the key
  with other text, silence, or another stage's key.
- Approval unlocks Test Design. It never starts it.

## Revision Rules

1. Update the **same** ExplorationReport.md. Never create `-v2` or `-final`
   copies.
2. Bump the report's minor version, and record the reason and changed sections
   in the Revision History.
3. Re-explore **only** what the revision needs, and capture new evidence with
   new `EVD-###` IDs. Keep earlier evidence files.
4. Keep IDs stable. A withdrawn observation keeps its ID, is marked withdrawn,
   and its ID is never reused.
5. A reviewer's clarification can change a classification. Cite it as
   `Reviewer clarification (journal EVT-NNNN)`. If it changes documented
   expectations, recommend a ProjectContext revision instead of editing it.
6. Present the revision and STOP. `EXPLORATION_APPROVED` is required again.
   Changing an approved report voids its approval.

## Journal Requirements

- For every attempt, change request and gate reply, append an event conforming
  to `platform/schemas/journal-event.schema.json`.
- Record:
  - the verbatim trigger message,
  - the inputs with their hashes,
  - every Playwright MCP action actually performed, in order,
  - observations with their classifications and evidence links,
  - candidate locators, coverage, decisions and validation results,
  - the stop reason and the gate state.
- Never fabricate timestamps, hashes, actions, evidence or results. Use `null`
  plus a note when a value is unavailable.
- If Playwright MCP was not used (e.g. exploration was blocked), record
  "MCP: Not used" with the actual reason.

Prompt text (10,939 characters)
---
prompt_id: test-design
prompt_version: 1.0.0
skill: test-design

# Prompt — Test Design

> Versioning rule: any change to the text of this file requires a
> `prompt_version` bump. The journal identifies the prompt used for each run by
> `prompt_id` + `prompt_version`, and stores one verbatim snapshot of each version
> with its SHA-256 in the task's `journal/prompts.json`.

## Purpose

Turn **approved documented scope** and **approved observed behavior** into
concrete, executable, traceable test cases. Use recognized design techniques
rather than intuition, so coverage is defensible and the gaps are visible, not
accidental. This is a **design** stage: nothing is executed.

```
approved ProjectContext.md + approved ExplorationReport.md → TestCases.md → HUMAN REVIEW → TEST_DESIGN_APPROVED
```

## Expected Inputs

| Input | Location | Role |
|---|---|---|
| Approved Project Context | `{{task_path}}/context/ProjectContext.md` | Documented scope and business rules (`PC SA/BR/EB/DEP/RK/GAP/OQ-###`) |
| Approved Exploration Report | `{{task_path}}/exploration/ExplorationReport.md` | Observed behavior (`OBS/FND/XGAP/XQ/PB/XDEP-###`), coverage limits |
| Task identity | `{{task_path}}/context/TaskContext.md` § Identity | Identity only |
| PRD | `{{task_path}}/prd/PRD.md` | PRD IDs where traceability requires them |
| Constraints | Environment, test-data and role constraints from the inputs or the human | Feasibility and gaps |
| workflow_run_id | `{{workflow_run_id}}` (orchestration-owned, read only) | Header and journal |

## Prerequisites

Do not design anything unless all of the following hold:

- The task, TaskContext.md, PRD.md, ProjectContext.md and ExplorationReport.md exist.
- ProjectContext is approved with `PROJECT_CONTEXT_APPROVED`, and that approval
  is current.
- ExplorationReport is approved with `EXPLORATION_APPROVED`, and that approval is
  current.
- The approved exploration was performed against the currently approved
  ProjectContext.
- An active `workflow_run_id` exists.

Otherwise stop and report which prerequisite failed.

## Source-of-Truth Rules

- **ProjectContext** is documented scope and business rules. **ExplorationReport**
  is observed behavior. **Test Design** is coverage derived from both.
- ExplorationReport never replaces ProjectContext. Undocumented behavior is
  never a confirmed requirement.
- **Never invent an expected result.** An expected result must be supported by:
  1. ProjectContext (a PC BR or PC EB),
  2. approved exploration that is **consistent** with documented behavior (a
     MATCH observation can supply concrete detail, such as an exact message text
     or option list, for a documented behavior), or
  3. an explicit documented business rule.
- If none of these supports an expected result, the case is **Exploratory**
  and/or the point becomes an **Open Question**. Write the Expected Result exactly
  as `Not established by approved requirements.` and state what must be
  clarified.
- **FINDING:** the expected result stays the *documented* expectation, and the
  case notes the known finding.
- **GAP / QUESTION observations:** the case becomes Exploratory and/or an Open
  Question.
- **POTENTIAL BUG:** use the documented expectation if one exists. Otherwise the
  case is Exploratory. A potential bug is never treated as confirmed.

## Test Design Instructions

For each in-scope behavior or rule:

1. identify it (PC BR / PC EB / OBS),
2. decide which technique is appropriate,
3. apply the technique,
4. explain why it was selected,
5. generate the **minimum defensible** set of cases,
6. verify coverage.

Do **not** apply every technique blindly. For any technique not applied,
record why.

### Techniques

- **Equivalence Partitioning (EP):** partition inputs into logically equivalent
  classes (valid, invalid, missing, malformed, …). For each partition, record
  the partition, a representative value, the expected behavior and the source.
  Do not add redundant cases without justification.
- **Boundary Value Analysis (BVA):** for every documented, or approved-observed,
  numeric, length, date, quantity or similar limit, use just below, at and just
  above the boundary. Where applicable, also use min, min+1, max−1, max and max+1.
  Only use boundaries supported by approved sources.
- **Decision Tables (DT):** use these where behavior depends on multiple
  conditions. Record the conditions, condition values, resulting actions, the
  combinations covered, and the test case IDs. Collapse combinations whose
  outcomes are equivalent, and justify doing so.
- **State Transition Testing (ST):** use this for lifecycle or state behavior.
  Cover valid transitions, and the invalid transitions that sources establish.
  Never invent states or transitions.
- **Pairwise / Combinatorial (PW):** use this for many independent parameters.
  Record the dimensions, values, strategy, selected combinations and the coverage
  achieved. Never claim exhaustive coverage for pairwise.
- **Error Guessing (EG):** supplementary only. It never replaces a systematic
  technique. Candidate areas: missing required data, malformed input, repeated
  submission, unexpected sequence, timeout, duplicate action, invalid state,
  stale data. Record the rationale. The expected result still needs an approved
  source, or the case is Exploratory.

## Coverage Rules

- Cover these types where applicable:
  1. Positive
  2. Negative
  3. Boundary
  4. Validation (each field rule, including the exact message where it is
     established)
  5. Business Rule (each PC BR individually)
  6. Dependency
  7. Error Handling
  8. Exploratory
  9. End-to-End (the full realistic workflow)
- Every applicable **PC BR** has at least one covering case, or an explicit
  Coverage Gap. Every **PC EB** is covered or has a gap.
- Consider every **Exploration observation**.
  - FINDING and POTENTIAL BUG observations need a covering case or a gap.
  - GAP and QUESTION observations need an Exploratory case and/or an Open
    Question.
  - An observation that needs no case is listed with the reason.
- Exploration coverage limits are carried into Coverage Gaps.

## Test Case Rules

- Each case records:
  - ID `TC-###`, Title, Type, Priority (+ basis)
  - Preconditions, Test Data (`DATA-###` refs), ordered Steps
  - **one single unambiguous Expected Result**, and its Expected Result Basis
  - Traceability, Technique (`TA-###`)
  - Valid in Scope (+ reason), Needs Automation (+ rationale)
  - Status, Notes
- Split independent expectations into separate cases.
- Cases are independent, repeatable, executable and deterministic where
  possible. Preconditions set up their own state, and nothing relies on
  execution order.
- Steps use only screens, fields and flows established by ProjectContext or
  approved exploration. Never invent UI elements.
- **Priority:** High, Medium or Low, based on documented business importance,
  risk (PC RK), critical-workflow impact, or explicit project context. Record the
  basis. If no basis exists, write `Not established`. Use a project-defined
  default only if one exists.
- **Valid in Scope:** TRUE when the case exercises an in-scope PC area and does
  not depend on excluded functionality. Otherwise FALSE, with a reason and a
  matching Coverage Gap.
- **Needs Automation:** TRUE only when all of these hold:
  - Valid in Scope is TRUE,
  - the expected result is established (not Exploratory),
  - the case is deterministic and repeatable,
  - the case has regression value.

  Otherwise FALSE, with a rationale. Exploratory cases are always FALSE.
- IDs are stable: `TC-001`, `TC-002`, … An edited case keeps its ID. A removed case
  is marked withdrawn with the reason, and its ID is never reused or renumbered.
- **Source IDs:** reuse upstream IDs exactly, qualified as `PC BR-001`,
  `PC EB-003` or `PRD FR-002`. Exploration IDs are used as they are (`OBS-014`,
  `FND-002`). Never invent a source ID.

## Exploratory Cases, Open Questions, Gaps, Test Data

- **Exploratory cases:** only where behavior is undocumented, the expected result
  is not established, exploration found a GAP, or further investigation is
  needed. Each one records the case ID, why it is exploratory, the observed
  behavior, what is unknown, the question (`TQ-###`), and the evidence or source
  references. Never convert a GAP into a guessed expected result.
- **Open Questions (`TQ-###`):** the related source, related cases, the exact
  ambiguity, and the required clarification. Owner is `not stated` unless given.
  Questions stay open until a human or business decision is provided.
- **Coverage Gaps (`CG-###`):** uncovered business rules, uncovered observations,
  inaccessible functionality, missing test data, unavailable roles, environment
  limitations and intentional exclusions. Each gap records the reason, impact
  (if known), dependency or blocker, and whether follow-up is required. Never
  hide missing coverage.
- **Test Data (`DATA-###`):** the data required for execution, such as users,
  roles, dates, ranges, account states and dependent records. Include its
  category, the cases that use it, and its source.
  - Never include real credentials or secrets. Reference the configured test-data
    source instead.
  - Synthetic values use reserved domains (e.g. `example.com`, `.test`).

## Output Structure

Write `{{task_path}}/test-design/TestCases.md` following
`.claude/skills/test-design/TestCases.template.md`, with these sections:

1. Test Design Information
2. Scope
3. Test Design Strategy
4. Test Cases
5. Traceability Matrix
6. Technique Rationale (with technique tables)
7. Exploratory Cases
8. Open Questions
9. Coverage Gaps
10. Test Data Requirements
11. Coverage Summary

§11 gives counts only, with **no** quality score.

## Stop Conditions

After writing TestCases.md: **STOP** and present it for review. Do **not**:

- execute any case, open a browser, or use Playwright MCP,
- generate automation (Selenium, SHAFT, TestNG, Playwright or other code),
- report pass/fail results, or create bugs,
- start Manual Execution or Automation.

## Approval Requirements

- The only progression key is the exact, case-sensitive, standalone value:

  ```
  TEST_DESIGN_APPROVED
  ```

- These are **not** approval: `approved`, `looks good`, `continue`, `proceed`,
  `LGTM`, `TEST_DESIGN_APPROVED please continue`, any message containing the key
  with other text, silence, or another stage's key.
- **Revisions:**
  - update the same TestCases.md (never `-v2` or `-final`),
  - keep the existing case IDs,
  - bump the minor version,
  - record the reason, the changed sections, and the added, removed and
    modified cases,
  - STOP, and require `TEST_DESIGN_APPROVED` again.

  Changing an approved TestCases.md voids its approval.

# Prompt — Test Review

> Versioning rule: any change to the text of this file requires a
> `prompt_version` bump. The journal identifies the prompt used for each run by
> `prompt_id` + `prompt_version`, and stores one verbatim snapshot of each version
> with its SHA-256 in the task's `journal/prompts.json`.

## Purpose

Review the **exact approved version** of the task's test cases before anything
is executed or automated. Check them against the **approved documented scope**
and the **approved observed behavior**. Record every problem as a finding with a
proposed change and its reason. **Never modify the test cases.** This is a
**review** stage: nothing is executed.

```
approved TestCases.md (exact SHA-256) + approved ProjectContext.md + approved ExplorationReport.md
  → TestReview.md → HUMAN REVIEW → TEST_REVIEW_APPROVED (only with no open Change Required finding)
```

## Expected Inputs

| Input | Location | Role |
|---|---|---|
| Approved Test Cases | `{{task_path}}/test-design/TestCases.md` | **The reviewed artifact.** Read only. |
| Approved Project Context | `{{task_path}}/context/ProjectContext.md` | Documented scope and business rules (`PC SA/BR/EB/DEP/RK/AS/GAP/OQ-###`) |
| Approved Exploration Report | `{{task_path}}/exploration/ExplorationReport.md` | Observed behavior (`OBS/FND/XGAP/XQ/PB/XDEP-###`), coverage limits |
| Task identity | `{{task_path}}/context/TaskContext.md` § Identity | Identity only |
| PRD | `{{task_path}}/prd/PRD.md` | Traceability chains only |
| Previous review | `{{task_path}}/test-review/TestReview.md` | Re-reviews and revisions only: finding IDs and history |
| workflow_run_id | `{{workflow_run_id}}` (orchestration-owned, read only) | Header and journal |

## Prerequisites

Do not review anything unless all of the following hold:

- The task, TaskContext.md, PRD.md, ProjectContext.md, ExplorationReport.md and
  TestCases.md exist, and TestCases.md is not the template placeholder.
- ProjectContext, ExplorationReport and TestCases.md are approved with
  `PROJECT_CONTEXT_APPROVED`, `EXPLORATION_APPROVED` and `TEST_DESIGN_APPROVED`,
  and each approval is current.
- The approved exploration was performed against the current approved
  ProjectContext, and the approved test design was produced against the current
  approved ProjectContext and ExplorationReport.
- An active `workflow_run_id` exists.

Otherwise stop and report which prerequisite failed.

## Binding

Before reviewing, record the SHA-256, version and approval event of TestCases.md,
ProjectContext.md and ExplorationReport.md. Hash TestCases.md again at the end.
If the two TestCases.md hashes differ, stop: the review is invalid. The approval
of this review will apply **only** to the TestCases.md hash recorded here.

## Review Instructions

Review **every active test case** against all eight checks, and verify **every
applicable source** for coverage:

1. **Missing coverage:** every applicable PC BR and PC EB, and every FINDING,
   POTENTIAL BUG, GAP and QUESTION observation, has a covering case, a TQ or a
   coverage gap (CG). Check that each matrix claim is real: the case's steps and
   expected result actually exercise the source. Dependencies (PC DEP, XDEP) and
   Exploration coverage limits are covered or listed as gaps.
2. **Duplicates:** cases with the same preconditions, data partition, steps and
   expected outcome that add no distinct coverage.
3. **Unclear steps:** ambiguous, unordered, or missing values; or using a screen,
   field, control or flow that ProjectContext and approved exploration do not
   establish.
4. **Invalid expected results:** no approved basis; contradicts ProjectContext;
   uses FINDING-observed behavior as the expectation; more than one expectation;
   a `Not established` case not typed Exploratory or not linked to a TQ; an
   Exploratory case whose expected result is not exactly
   `Not established by approved requirements.` Judge against the test-design
   Expected Result Rules.
5. **Missing preconditions:** state, role, record or data the case needs but does
   not set up; any dependency on another case running first.
6. **Unsupported assumptions:** an assumption, limit, state, transition, role or
   value that no approved source supports, or reliance on an unconfirmed
   assumption without saying so.
7. **Traceability errors:** a cited source ID that does not exist upstream;
   disagreement between the two traceability directions; coverage counts that do
   not match the case index; missing OBS or TQ links; a FALSE Valid in Scope
   without a CG.
8. **Flag inconsistencies:** Needs Automation TRUE on an Exploratory,
   `Not established`, non-deterministic or out-of-scope case; Valid in Scope TRUE
   on a case that depends on excluded functionality; a flag without a reason.

Report each category as performed, with its count, or as not applicable with the
reason.

## Source-of-Truth Rules

- ProjectContext is documented scope and business rules. ExplorationReport is
  observed behavior. TestCases.md is the artifact under review, never a source of
  truth about the application.
- Every finding cites the approved source IDs or the TestCases sections it rests
  on. Never cite an ID that does not exist.
- **Never invent application behavior.** If the approved sources do not establish
  the right expected result, say so. The proposed change is then "mark
  Exploratory / raise a TQ", never a guessed expected result.

## Findings

Record each problem as `TR-###` with: classification, category, affected cases
or sources, basis, **proposed change** (what must change, and where), **reason**
(why, citing the source or rule), and resolution status.

- **Change Required** blocks approval. Use it whenever the problem, left
  unresolved, would make an execution or automation result untrustworthy, or
  violates a platform rule. This always includes:
  - an invalid expected result,
  - a flag inconsistency,
  - an applicable source with neither a case nor a CG, or a false matrix claim,
  - a step that two testers could perform differently or cannot perform as written,
  - a missing precondition the result depends on, or an execution-order dependency,
  - an invented source ID or a wrong count,
  - an assumption that decides the steps or the expected result,
  - duplicates that contradict each other.

  If unsure, classify Change Required and state the doubt.
- **Advisory** does not block approval. Use it for improvements that do not
  change how a case is performed or judged: clearer wording, consistent
  duplicates to consolidate, an optional extra case where a CG already
  documents the gap, a weak priority basis.
- **Never** edit TestCases.md, write a replacement case, or apply a proposed
  change. Changes are made only through a `test-design` revision.
- **Never** withdraw a Change Required finding or downgrade it to Advisory unless
  the human explicitly decided so in a change request. Cite that request.

## Re-Review

When TestCases.md has a new approved version:

- re-bind to its new hash,
- re-run all eight checks over the whole file (not only the changes),
- re-assess every earlier finding: `resolved` (state what was verified in the new
  version), or still `open`,
- give new findings the next unused `TR-###`, and never renumber or reuse an ID.

## Output Structure

Write `{{task_path}}/test-review/TestReview.md` following
`.claude/skills/test-review/TestReview.template.md`, with these sections:

1. Review Information (including the binding)
2. Review Scope
3. Review Checks
4. Per-Case Review
5. Source Coverage Verification
6. Findings
7. Open Questions
8. Resolution Tracking
9. Review Summary

§9 gives counts and the review outcome only, with **no** quality score.

## Stop Conditions

After writing TestReview.md: **STOP** and present it for review. If any Change
Required finding is open, state that the review cannot be approved until it is
resolved through a test-design revision, or withdrawn or downgraded by the human.
Do **not**:

- modify TestCases.md or any other upstream artifact,
- execute any case, open a browser, or use Playwright MCP,
- generate automation in any framework, report pass/fail results, or create bugs,
- start Test Design, Manual Execution or Automation Generation.

## Approval Requirements

- The only progression key is the exact, case-sensitive, standalone value:

  ```
  TEST_REVIEW_APPROVED
  ```

- These are **not** approval: `approved`, `looks good`, `continue`, `proceed`,
  `LGTM`, `TEST_REVIEW_APPROVED please continue`, any message containing the key
  with other text, silence, `TEST_DESIGN_APPROVED`, or another stage's key.
- The exact key is **refused** while any Change Required finding is open, or if
  TestCases.md, TestReview.md, ProjectContext.md or ExplorationReport.md changed
  since the review. Say which, and what is needed.
- The approval applies only to the TestCases.md hash bound in §1. Any later change
  to TestCases.md or TestReview.md voids it.
- **Revisions:**
  - update the same TestReview.md (never `-v2` or `-final`),
  - keep the existing finding IDs,
  - bump the minor version,
  - record the reason and the findings added, changed, resolved or withdrawn,
  - STOP, and require `TEST_REVIEW_APPROVED` again.
